INTERNATIONAL
India's Entry into the PAX Silica Strategic Alliance
• In February 2026, India joined the "PAX Silica" alliance led by the United States, enhancing cooperation
in critical minerals and artificial intelligence (AI).
• The agreement was signed in the presence of Union Minister Ashwini Vaishnaw and US Ambassador
Sergio Cortez during the India AI Impact Summit held at the Bharat Mandapam in New Delhi.
• As a capability-based alliance, PAX Silica focuses on securing supply chains for critical minerals needed
for semiconductor manufacturing, promoting AI research and innovation, and ensuring reliable
technological partnerships.
• The alliance strengthens the India-US strategic partnership and aligns with India's goal of becoming a
global hub for semiconductor manufacturing and AI development.
• Washington D.C., the capital of the United States, uses the US Dollar as its currency; the Ministry of
Electronics and Information Technology, established in 2016, is based in New Delhi; the Bharat
Mandapam in Pragati Maidan hosted the 2023 G20 Summit.
Host Countries for Major Global Summits in 2026
• Host countries for 2026: BRICS - India, SCO - Kyrgyzstan, ASEAN - Philippines, G20 - United States,
G7 - France, APEC - China, NATO - Turkey, COP - Turkey.
2026 G7 Summit (52nd Summit)
• French President Emmanuel Macron extended an invitation to Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi for
the 52nd G7 Summit (June 15-17, 2026, Évian-les-Bains, Haute-Savoie).
• Macron issued the invitation during his visit to Mumbai on February 17, 2026, where he discussed India’s
role in the summit and in preparatory discussions.
• The G7 summit serves as an informal political and economic platform for developed democracies to
coordinate on global economic stability, security, climate-energy transitions, and geopolitical challenges.
• G7 member countries: United States, United Kingdom, France, Germany, Italy, Japan, Canada; the
European Union participates as a non-enumerated member through institutional leadership.
• The first G7 (G6) leaders’ summit was held in Rambouillet (France) in 1975, and the current G7 format
was established when Canada joined in 1976; the 52nd summit will be hosted by France from June 15-17,
2026, in Évian-les-Bains.
Trusted Tech Alliance for Digital Transparency
• 15 global tech companies from Africa, Asia, Europe, and North America formed the Trusted Tech Alliance
(TTA) and announced it during the Munich Security Conference (MSC 2026) held from February 13-15,
2026.
• The TTA's primary goal is to create joint, verifiable standards for transparency, security, governance, and
data protection across key digital layers like connectivity, cloud infrastructure, semiconductors, software,
and AI. The alliance emphasizes trust based on transparency-security practices rather than the country of
the supplier.
• Founding members include companies like Anthropic, AWS, Cassava Technologies, Cohere, Ericsson,
Google Cloud, Hanwha, Jio Platforms, Microsoft, Nokia, Nscale, NTT, Rapidus, Saab, and SAP. TTA
defines its commitment to transparent corporate governance, ethical conduct, operational transparency,
secure development, independent evaluation, supply chain security, rule of law, and data protection
through five key principles.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 2 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 3 ===
• With Jio Platforms as a founding member, India's role in shaping global standards in connectivity, cloud,
semiconductors, and AI is strengthened. Jio CEO Kiran Thomas emphasized that through trusted, secure,
and transparent technology, Jio is committed to advancing unified standards and verifiable practices across
the tech stack for comprehensive digital growth.
• The TTA was launched in 2026 as a multi-company international alliance; the Munich Security
Conference, held annually in Munich (Germany) since 1963, is a major international security policy
conference; Jio Platforms, established in 2019, is a Reliance Group technology-connectivity company
based in Ghansoli (Navi Mumbai), with Kiran Thomas as CEO.
Maritime Labour Convention, 2006 – 20 Years of the ‘Seafarers’ Bill of Rights’
• The International Labour Organization (ILO) and the International Maritime Organization (IMO)
celebrated the 20th anniversary of the adoption of the Maritime Labour Convention (MLC), 2006, marking
it as the "Seafarers’ Bill of Rights."
• The MLC was approved on February 23, 2006, during the International Labour Conference held in
Geneva. It consolidated over 60 previous ILO maritime labour conventions into a single, comprehensive
document, setting minimum standards for the working and living conditions of seafarers.
• Goals: To ensure dignified work and living conditions for seafarers, irrespective of nationality or ship’s
flag; to reduce unfair competition from substandard ships; and to ensure equal standards in the maritime
industry. It also strengthened the enforcement system through Flag State–Port State inspections, covering
minimum qualifications, employment conditions (wages, working/resting hours, leave, repatriation),
accommodation, medical services, social security, and more.
• ILO, based in Geneva (Switzerland), was established in 1919 and operates through a tripartite structure
with government, employer, and worker participation; IMO, based in London (United Kingdom), focuses
on maritime safety and pollution control.
• Switzerland’s capital is Bern, with the currency Swiss Franc (CHF); the United Kingdom’s capital is
London, with the currency Pound Sterling (GBP). The MLC, 2006 is considered the "fourth pillar" in
international maritime law and is seen as a key instrument for strengthening the rights, welfare, and fair
competition for seafarers.
Digital Transparency for Trusted Tech Alliance
• 15 global tech companies from Africa, Asia, Europe, and North America formed the Trusted Tech Alliance
(TTA) and announced it during the Munich Security Conference (MSC 2026) held from February 13-15,
2026, in Munich, Germany.
• The primary goal of TTA is to develop joint, verifiable standards for transparency, security, governance,
and data protection across key digital areas, such as connectivity, cloud infrastructure, semiconductors,
software, and AI. The alliance promotes a perspective that trust is based on transparency-security practices
rather than the country of the supplier.
• Founding members of TTA include companies such as Anthropic, AWS, Cassava Technologies, Cohere,
Ericsson, Google Cloud, Hanwha, Jio Platforms, Microsoft, Nokia, Nscale, NTT, Rapidus, Saab, and SAP.
The alliance defines its commitment to transparent corporate governance, ethical conduct, operational
transparency, secure development, independent evaluation, supply chain security, rule of law, and data
protection through five principles.
• With Jio Platforms as a founding member, India’s role in shaping global standards for connectivity, cloud,
semiconductors, and AI is strengthened. Jio CEO Kiran Thomas emphasized that Jio is committed to
advancing unified standards and verifiable practices across the entire tech stack for comprehensive digital
growth through reliable, secure, and transparent technology.

India–West Asia ‘Shadbhujii Coalition’ Proposal – Statement by Israel's Prime Minister Netanyahu
• Israel’s Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu announced the creation of a ‘Shadbhujii Coalition,’ involving
India and West Asian countries, with additional participation from Arab, African, and Asian nations.
• Netanyahu emphasized that Greece and Cyprus in the Mediterranean region would also be part of this
coalition, and its main objective is to strengthen regional strategic cooperation, enhance partnerships in
economic, security, and connectivity sectors.
• This coalition is seen as a signal for strategic alignment between India, Israel, and the Mediterranean,
serving as a new platform linked to existing connectivity initiatives like the IMEC, announced at the 2023
G20 Summit.
• Benjamin Netanyahu is the longest-serving Prime Minister in Israeli history; Israel's capital is Jerusalem,
and its currency is the Israeli New Shekel (ILS); the President is Isaac Herzog, and the Prime Minister is
Benjamin Netanyahu.
• India, Israel, UAE, and the USA are members of the I2U2; IMEC was announced at the 2023 G20 Summit;
West Asia plays a key role in India's energy security.
India–Israel Bilateral Relations – Special Strategic Partnership
• The Indian Prime Minister visited Israel on a historic official tour and announced that the two countries
had expanded their relations into a special strategic partnership focused on peace, innovation, and
prosperity.
• India and Israel share multifaceted relations, notably in the security sector, with strong cooperation in
technology and innovation, continuing collaboration, and a shared vision for regional stability,
transforming limited diplomatic ties into a strong strategic partnership.
• This partnership is deepening cooperation in defense, agriculture, water management, cyber security, and
advanced technological sectors, relying on mutual strategic benefits and ancient civilizational ties.
• In agriculture, more than 1 million Indian farmers have been trained through 35 Indo-Israel Centers of
Excellence; joint research funds have increased under India-Israel's collaborative research initiatives, and
advanced tech projects are being promoted through the I4F industry research fund.
• Under labor migration, there is a target to send 50,000 additional Indian workers to the construction and
nursing sectors in the next five years; following the 2025 bilateral investment agreement, free trade
agreement talks will accelerate, strengthening commercial and investment ties.
• Israel's capital is Jerusalem, with the currency the Israeli New Shekel; the President is Isaac Herzog, and
the Prime Minister is Benjamin Netanyahu. Key historical facts: India recognized Israel in 1950,
established full diplomatic relations in 1992, and defense cooperation remains the core pillar, with
agriculture, water management, cyber security, and modern tech cooperation rapidly increasing.
India Joins BRICS Industrial Competence Centre
• India joined the BRICS Industrial Competence Centre to provide comprehensive support to manufacturing
companies and micro, small, and medium enterprises (MSMEs) in BRICS countries. The National
Productivity Council (NPC) has been appointed as the nodal agency to lead the coordination for India.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 4 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 5 ===
• The Centre, which was established in 2024–25 at the United Nations Industrial Development Organization
(UNIDO) headquarters, functions as a government-private partnership platform under the BRICS New
Industrial Revolution Partnership, supporting the adoption of advanced manufacturing, Industry 4.0,
digital technologies, and sustainable practices.
• The primary goals include enhancing the competitiveness of the manufacturing sector and MSMEs,
improving productivity, facilitating digital transformation, promoting partnerships among BRICS
countries, technical suppliers, and research institutions, and improving market access, expansion, funding
availability, skills development, and industry modernization.
• India’s participation increases the opportunities for Indian manufacturing and MSMEs to integrate into the
BRICS value chains, aligning with the ‘Make in India’ and ‘Atmanirbhar Bharat’ goals.
• BRICS – Established in 2009; Nature: Intergovernmental grouping of emerging economies; Member
countries: Brazil, Russia, India, China, South Africa, Saudi Arabia, UAE, Iran, Ethiopia, Indonesia, Egypt;
Goal: Economic cooperation and global governance reforms.
• National Productivity Council (NPC) – Established: 1958; Headquarters: New Delhi; Under the Ministry
of Commerce & Industry.
• UNIDO (United Nations Industrial Development Organization) – Established: 1966; Headquarters:
Vienna, Austria.
Arab League (League of Arab States)
• India’s Foreign Minister recently met with foreign ministers from five member states of the Arab League,
discussing ways to strengthen relations with West Asia and regional issues, further enhancing India-Arab
ties.
• The Arab League (League of Arab States) is a voluntary regional organization of Arabic-speaking or
Arabic-majority countries, aiming to coordinate political, economic, social, and cultural policies among
Arab countries in West Asia and Africa, promoting collective interests.
• Established on March 22, 1945, in Cairo (Egypt), the founding members were Egypt, Iraq, Transjordan
(later Jordan), Lebanon, Saudi Arabia, and Syria; Yemen joined on May 5, 1945.
• The current member countries are 22 – Algeria, Bahrain, Comoros, Djibouti, Egypt, Iraq, Jordan, Kuwait,
Lebanon, Libya, Mauritania, Morocco, Oman, Palestine, Qatar, Saudi Arabia, Somalia, Sudan, Syria,
Tunisia, United Arab Emirates, Yemen.
• Observer member countries: Brazil, Eritrea, India, Venezuela.
• The headquarters is in Cairo (Egypt), and the Arab League is one of the oldest regional intergovernmental
organizations globally.
• The current Secretary-General is : Ahmed Aboul Gheit.
• Egypt Capital: Cairo; Currency: Egyptian Pound; President: Abdel Fattah el-Sisi.
• Saudi Arabia Capital: Riyadh; Currency: Saudi Riyal; King: Salman bin Abdulaziz Al Saud.
• Iraq Capital: Baghdad; Currency: Iraqi Dinar; President: Abdul Latif Rashid.
• Kuwait Capital: Kuwait City; Currency: Kuwaiti Dinar; Emir: Mishal Al-Ahmad Al-Jaber Al-Sabah.
• UAE Capital: Abu Dhabi; Currency: UAE Dirham; President: Mohamed bin Zayed Al Nahyan.
• India’s status as an observer member of the Arab League enhances its strategic, economic, energy, and
diaspora connections with West Asia, further expanding cooperation through platforms like the India–
Arab League Dialogue and the Arab–India Cooperation Forum.

International Climate Initiative (IKI) – India-Germany Climate Partnership
• Germany and India have launched a €20 million (approximately ₹180 crore) large grant project under the
International Climate Initiative (IKI), aimed at strengthening India's climate resilience.
• IKI is a key climate financing mechanism of the German Federal Government, funding international
projects related to climate change mitigation, adaptation, REDD+, and biodiversity conservation,
primarily supporting developing countries.
• Established in 2008, IKI currently operates in over 150 countries. India, Brazil, China, South Africa,
Mexico, and Indonesia are among the 14 priority countries.
• IKI supports the implementation and expansion of Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs)
submitted under the Paris Agreement, as well as the achievement of goals set under the Convention on
Biological Diversity (CBD).
• International Climate Initiative (IKI) – Headquarters: Berlin, Germany; Established: 2008; Funding
Source: German Federal Government; Key Areas: Climate Mitigation, Adaptation, REDD+, Biodiversity
Conservation.
• Germany Capital: Berlin; Currency: Euro (€); President: Frank-Walter Steinmeier; Chancellor: Friedrich
Merz; one of the main global donors to climate funds.
• Key Exam Information: IKI is Germany’s primary bilateral climate finance mechanism; India is one of
the 14 priority countries; NDCs are a key part of the Paris Agreement; Climate Resilience refers to the
capacity to withstand climate impacts.
India Signs Five-Year Agreement with World Food Programme
• India has signed a five-year agreement with the United Nations World Food Programme (WFP) to support
global hunger alleviation efforts, particularly in Africa, West Asia, and conflict-affected regions.
• The agreement includes a supply of up to 200,000 metric tons of rice, with up to 25% being broken grains,
to support emergency food relief efforts.
• The contract is designed to ensure timely delivery of food grains for urgent humanitarian assistance, with
the option to extend the agreement beyond the five-year period.
• This collaboration strengthens India's role in global food security and humanitarian cooperation.
• World Food Programme (WFP) – Established: 1961; Headquarters: Rome, Italy; Executive Director:
Cindy McCain; Nature: UN Humanitarian Agency; Nobel Peace Prize: 2020.
• Food Corporation of India (FCI) – Established: 1965; Headquarters: New Delhi; Chairman and MD:
Ravindra Kumar Agrawal; Under the Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food & Public Distribution.
• Italy Capital: Rome; Currency: Euro (€); Prime Minister: Giorgia Meloni.
• Humanitarian food assistance refers to providing emergency food support during wars, natural disasters,
and economic crises.
India-Nepal Environmental Awareness Agreement
• On February 25, 2026, the Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change, India, and the Ministry
of Forests and Environment, Nepal, signed a Memorandum of Understanding in New Delhi.
• The agreement aims to strengthen bilateral cooperation in the areas of forestry, wildlife, environmental
conservation, biodiversity preservation, and climate change.
• Union Minister Bhupender Yadav (India) and Nepal's Minister Madhav Prasad Choulagain signed the
agreement.

• The focus will be on joint conservation of natural resources along border areas, sustainable management,
and preservation of ecosystems and wildlife habitats extending across both countries.
• Cooperation will be enhanced to tackle climate change impacts, along with the conservation of the
Himalayan and Terai regions.
• Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change – Headquarters: New Delhi; Established: 1985;
Minister: Bhupender Yadav; Responsible for environmental policies and forest conservation.
• Nepal Capital: Kathmandu; Currency: Nepalese Rupee; President: Ram Chandra Poudel; Prime Minister:
Sushila Karki.
India-Nepal: Mutual Legal Assistance Treaty on Criminal Matters
• On February 18, 2026, India and Nepal governments signed a Mutual Legal Assistance Treaty (MLAT)
on criminal matters in Kathmandu, Nepal. This agreement strengthens bilateral legal cooperation and
addresses cross-border crimes.
• The agreement was signed by Nepal's Joint Secretary Binod Kumar Bhattarai and India's Ambassador to
Nepal Naveen Srivastava.
• The main objective is to improve coordination on international and cross-border crimes, and establish
effective cooperation between legal systems.
• It provides a legal framework for mutual exchange of information and evidence related to criminal
investigations and prosecutions, and coordination in judicial processes.
• The treaty includes issues such as financial fraud, money laundering, terrorism-related crimes, human
trafficking, and cybercrimes.
• Both countries will designate a central authority for sending and receiving legal assistance requests;
requests will be processed through official legal and diplomatic channels as per domestic laws.
• This agreement further strengthens India-Nepal security and legal cooperation.
• India-Nepal relations are based on the 1950 Peace and Friendship Treaty, with special ties between the
border and people.
• Nepal Capital: Kathmandu; President: Ram Chandra Poudel; Prime Minister: Sushila Karki.
• India Capital: New Delhi; Prime Minister: Narendra Modi; Foreign Minister: Dr. S. Jaishankar.
India-Brazil Agreement on Pharmaceutical and Medical Product Regulation
• In February 2026, India and Brazil signed an agreement to strengthen bilateral cooperation in the field of
pharmaceutical and medical product regulation.
• The agreement was signed between India's Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO) and
Brazil’s Health Regulatory Agency (ANVISA).
• The agreement documents were exchanged in the presence of Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi and
Brazilian President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva in New Delhi.
• The agreement aims to enhance coordination in regulatory policies, increase awareness of mutual
regulatory systems, and facilitate effective monitoring of drugs and medical products.
• The agreement ensures the availability of safe, effective, and quality-assured medicines and medical
products for both countries’ populations.
• This collaboration further strengthens the strategic partnership between India and Brazil in the health and
regulatory sectors.
• CDSCO – Established: 1940 under the Drugs and Cosmetics Act; Headquarters: New Delhi; Drugs
Controller General of India: Dr. Rajeev Singh Raghuvanshi.

• ANVISA – Established: 1999; Headquarters: Brasília, Brazil.
• Ministry of Health and Family Welfare – Established: 1947; Headquarters: New Delhi; Minister: Jagat

• Brazil Capital: Brasília; Currency: Brazilian Real; President: Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva.
India-UK Social Security Contributions Agreement
• India and the United Kingdom signed a Social Security Contributions Agreement on February 10, 2026,
in New Delhi.
• The agreement aims to avoid double social security contributions for employees working temporarily in
either country for up to 36 months.
• The agreement was signed by India’s Foreign Secretary Vikram Misri and the British High Commissioner
to India Lindy Cameron.
• Both countries agreed to finalize a similar social security agreement during the Comprehensive Economic
and Trade Agreement (CETA) negotiations in July 2025.
• Many countries enter such social security agreements to avoid double contributions in temporary foreign
postings.
• India already has social security agreements with countries like Germany, France, Belgium, Netherlands,
Switzerland, Sweden, Finland, Canada, South Korea, among others.
• United Kingdom Capital: London; Currency: Pound Sterling; Prime Minister: Sir Keir Starmer; Monarch:
Charles III.
European Union ‘Legal Gateway’ Office in India
• The European Union (EU) has set up a 'Legal Gateway' office in India, aimed at providing legal guidance
and assistance to those traveling from India to 27 EU member countries.
• The office will particularly assist students, researchers, and skilled professionals in facilitating legal
migration processes in a safe and lawful manner.
• This is the first 'Gateway' office established by the European Union outside its member countries.
• The office was inaugurated at an EU-led event in New Delhi, with Indian Foreign Minister Dr. S.
Jaishankar attending.
• This office will also help connect Indian talent to educational and employment opportunities in the
European Union, strengthening the India-EU strategic partnership.
• European Union (EU) Established: 1993; Headquarters: Brussels, Belgium; Member Countries: 27;
Nature: Political and Economic Union.
• President of the European Commission: Ursula von der Leyen.
• European Parliament – Main headquarters: Strasbourg and Brussels.
• Belgium Capital: Brussels; Currency: Euro (€); Prime Minister: Bart De Wever.
India-UK Offshore Wind Taskforce under Vision 2035
• On February 18, 2026, India and the UK jointly launched the India-UK Offshore Wind Taskforce during
the 4th India-UK Energy Dialogue in New Delhi.
• Indian Minister for New and Renewable Energy Pralhad Joshi and UK Foreign Secretary David Lammy
participated in the launch. The taskforce was established under the India-UK Vision 2035 framework.
• The main goal is to accelerate the development of India’s offshore wind sector, ensure policy coordination,
regulatory reforms, and industry leadership.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 8 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 9 ===
• The taskforce aims to enhance long-term energy security and industrial competitiveness.
• Gujarat and Tamil Nadu have been identified as initial offshore wind development zones.
• The taskforce will support the expansion of renewable energy potential along the western and southern
coastlines; ₹7,453 crore has been allocated for Viability Gap Funding (VGF) for early-stage projects.
• The collaboration is structured around three main pillars – Ecosystem Planning, Infrastructure
Development, and Financial Structure.
• This initiative aligns with India's clean energy transition and Net Zero goals.
• By 2026, India’s installed non-fossil fuel electricity capacity surpassed 272 gigawatts, including over 141
gigawatts from solar power and 55 gigawatts from wind power.
• In FY 2025-26, over 35 gigawatts of solar power and 4.61 gigawatts of wind power were added.
• Ministry of New and Renewable Energy (MNRE) – Headquarters: New Delhi; Minister: Pralhad Joshi;
Responsible for implementing renewable energy policies.
• India-UK Vision 2035 – Adopted: 2021; Framework for bilateral strategic partnership in trade, defense,
climate change, technology, and more.
• United Kingdom – Capital: London; Currency: Pound Sterling (£); Prime Minister: Keir Starmer.
• Gujarat – Capital: Gandhinagar; Chief Minister: Bhupendra Patel; Governor: Acharya Devvrat.
• Tamil Nadu – Capital: Chennai; Chief Minister: M. K. Stalin; Governor: R. N. Ravi.
$70 Billion Gaza Reconstruction Package – President Donald J. Trump’s Announcement
• According to US President Donald J. Trump, nine countries in the "Peace Council" have agreed to provide
a total of $70 billion for the reconstruction and rehabilitation of Gaza.
• The announcement was made during a diplomatic meeting on Gaza's reconstruction and stabilization in
2026; the countries agreeing to contribute funds are Kazakhstan, Uzbekistan, United Arab Emirates,
Morocco, Bahrain, Qatar, Saudi Arabia, Azerbaijan, and Kuwait.
• Five countries have agreed to provide security forces, with the first deployment planned for the Rafah
region.
• These funds will be used for Gaza’s reconstruction, infrastructure development, and long-term stability;
Trump described it as an investment in peace and harmony in the Middle East.
• Preliminary estimates have indicated that Gaza's reconstruction will require approximately $70 billion.
• Although the US has announced it will contribute $10 billion to the "Peace Council," the specifics of how
the funds will be utilized have not been disclosed; the plan will be executed in coordination with the United
Nations (UN).
• United States – Capital: Washington D.C.; Currency: US Dollar; President: Donald J. Trump (In office
from January 20, 2025).
• Gaza Strip – Located on the eastern Mediterranean coast; governed by Hamas since 2007; major city:
Gaza City; the Rafah border is shared with Egypt.
• United Nations (UN) Founded: 1945; Headquarters: New York; Secretary-General: António Guterres.
• Saudi Arabia Capital: Riyadh; Currency: Saudi Riyal; King: Salman bin Abdulaziz Al Saud; Crown Prince
& Prime Minister: Mohammed bin Salman.
• United Arab Emirates Capital: Abu Dhabi; Currency: UAE Dirham; President: Mohamed bin Zayed Al
Nahyan; Prime Minister: Mohammed bin Rashid Al Maktoum.
• Qatar Capital: Doha; Currency: Qatari Rial; Emir: Tamim bin Hamad Al Thani; Prime Minister:
Mohammed bin Abdulrahman Al Thani.

India–Gulf Cooperation Council Free Trade Agreement Negotiations Reopened
• In February 2026, India and the six-member Gulf Cooperation Council (GCC) signed the Terms of
Reference for the Free Trade Agreement (FTA) in New Delhi, officially restarting negotiations for the
India-GCC FTA.
• The event was chaired by India’s Minister for Commerce and Industry, Piyush Goyal, while GCC’s Chief
Negotiator Raja Al Marzouqi represented the GCC.
• The Terms of Reference outlined the scope of the agreement, guiding principles, and negotiation methods.
• The proposed agreement aims to implement stable and reliable trade policies, especially enhancing market
access for Indian products in the food processing and petrochemical sectors.
• The FTA’s primary goal is to strengthen the long-term trade partnership between India and GCC countries.
• Gulf Cooperation Council (GCC) – Established: 1981; Headquarters: Riyadh, Saudi Arabia; Nature:
Regional intergovernmental political and economic union.
• Member countries: Saudi Arabia, United Arab Emirates, Qatar, Kuwait, Oman, Bahrain.
• GCC is one of the most crucial trade blocs for India; GCC countries are major suppliers of oil and natural
gas to India.
• India exports food products, engineering goods, textiles, chemicals, and pharmaceuticals to GCC
countries.
India-Netherlands Hydrogen Fellowship Programme Launched
• On February 6, 2026, India and the Netherlands launched the India-Netherlands Hydrogen Fellowship
Programme to strengthen bilateral cooperation in clean energy research. An academic partnership
agreement was also signed between the University of Groningen and 19 IITs in India.
• This program is coordinated by India’s Department of Science & Technology (DST).
• The goal is to foster joint research on green hydrogen technologies, facilitate researcher exchanges, and
promote research capabilities and skill development through knowledge sharing.
• Green hydrogen has been recognized by India as a key pillar in its clean energy transition. This partnership
aligns with energy transformation, carbon emission reduction, and sustainable development goals.
• Department of Science & Technology (DST) – Established: 1971; Headquarters: New Delhi; Responsible
for science and technology policies and research funding.
• Green Hydrogen – A low-carbon fuel produced through electrolysis using renewable energy; India plays
a key role in net-zero and clean energy transitions.

PM Modi Meets Indian Diaspora in Malaysia – Strengthening India-Malaysia Relations
• Prime Minister Narendra Modi arrived in Kuala Lumpur, Malaysia for a two-day visit and addressed the
Indian diaspora.
• He mentioned that the world views India as a reliable growth partner, with reliability becoming India’s
strong currency.
• This trust is reflected in agreements with the UK, UAE, Australia, New Zealand, the US, and the European
Union, he said.
• Modi noted that the India-Malaysia relationship is guided by the IMPACT Partnership, with both countries
moving forward hand-in-hand.
• He also stated that the Indian diaspora serves as a strong bridge between the two countries, highlighting
the deep cultural and linguistic ties between India and Malaysia.
• Prime Minister Modi spoke about Tamil as a great gift from India to the world, and Tamil literature is
timeless.
• He also shared a long-standing friendship with Malaysian Prime Minister Anwar Ibrahim, announcing the
establishment of the Thiruvalluvar Institute and a new Indian Consulate in Malaysia.
• Modi announced that the government will extend the Overseas Citizenship of India (OCI) cards to the 6th
generation of Indian diaspora.
• Malaysian Prime Minister Anwar Ibrahim welcomed Modi at the airport, and 800 artists performed a
spectacular welcome featuring Bharatanatyam, Kathakali, Kuchipudi, and Odissi, which was recorded in
the Malaysia Book of Records.
• The two leaders traveled to the venue in the same car, a special moment; discussions are expected on trade
and strategic relations between the two countries.
• India Capital: New Delhi; Prime Minister: Narendra Modi; President: Droupadi Murmu.
• Malaysia Capital: Kuala Lumpur; Prime Minister: Anwar Ibrahim; Head of State: Sultan Ibrahim.
• OCI (Overseas Citizenship of India) – Permanent residency status for the Indian diaspora; recent decision
extends it to the 6th generation.
India Signs Agreements with 23 Countries on Digital Public Infrastructure (DPI)
• In February 2026, India signed Memoranda of Understanding (MoUs) with 23 countries to share and
promote Digital Public Infrastructure (DPI) and India Stack solutions.
• This was announced by Jitin Prasada, Minister of State for Electronics and Information Technology
(MeitY), in Parliament.
• The MoUs primarily focus on enhancing cooperation in digital identity, digital payments, data exchange,
and service delivery platforms.
• Countries that signed agreements for DPI cooperation include Armenia, Sierra Leone, Suriname, Antigua
& Barbuda, Papua New Guinea, Trinidad & Tobago, Tanzania, Kenya, Cuba, Colombia, Laos, Saint Kitts
& Nevis, Ethiopia, Jamaica, Gambia, Fiji, Guyana, Venezuela, Sri Lanka, Brazil, Lesotho, Maldives, and
Mongolia.
• India’s UPI (Unified Payments Interface) is already available in countries like the UAE, Singapore,
Bhutan, Nepal, Sri Lanka, France, Mauritius, and Qatar.
• India has also signed agreements with Cuba, Kenya, UAE, and LPDR countries for cooperation on
DigiLocker.
• DigiLocker is India’s first digital document storage and verification platform.

• Through the India Stack Global Portal, partner countries can explore and adopt 18 key digital platforms,
including Aadhaar, UPI, CoWIN, and DigiLocker.
• These agreements will further strengthen India’s leadership in global digital governance.
• Digital Public Infrastructure (DPI)/India Stack – A set of digital platforms used for identification,
payments, data exchange, and service delivery; key components include Aadhaar, UPI, DigiLocker,
CoWIN, API Setu, UMANG.
• Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology (MeitY) – Established: 2016; Headquarters: New

India–Brazil Collaboration on Access to Traditional Knowledge Digital Library (TKDL)
• Brazil’s National Institute of Industrial Property (INPI) and India’s Council of Scientific and Industrial
Research (CSIR) have signed a cooperation agreement for access to the Traditional Knowledge Digital
Library (TKDL).
• The agreement allows Brazilian patent examiners to use information from the TKDL as a primary
reference, with the main aim of preventing wrongful patents on Indian traditional knowledge.
• The TKDL was established by the Indian government in 2001 and jointly developed by CSIR and the
Ministry of AYUSH.
• The library digitally records traditional medicinal principles from ancient texts, covering Ayurveda, Unani,
Siddha, Sowa-Rigpa, and Yoga systems.
• Information is translated in line with international patent classifications and is available to global patent
offices under non-disclosure agreements (NDAs).
• The TKDL serves as an intellectual property rights (IPR) tool to combat biopiracy and strengthens India-
Brazil collaboration on intellectual property.
• Traditional Knowledge Digital Library (TKDL) – Established: 2001; Developed by: CSIR & Ministry of
AYUSH; Purpose: To combat biopiracy; A database of thousands of medicinal formulas; Translated into
English, French, German, Spanish, and Japanese.
• CSIR – Established: 1942; Headquarters: New Delhi; Under the Ministry of Science and Technology;
President: The Prime Minister of India (Ex-officio).
• Ministry of AYUSH Headquarters: New Delhi; Established: 2014; Minister: Prataprao Jadhav.
• Brazil Capital: Brasília; Currency: Brazilian Real (BRL); President: Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva.
• Key Exam Information: TKDL was launched after patent disputes over turmeric, neem, etc.; it serves as a
global model for traditional knowledge protection; supports India's arguments in WTO-TRIPS
discussions.

systems.

Indian-Origin Climate Scientist Veerabhadran Ramanathan Wins Crawford Prize
• Indian-origin climate scientist Veerabhadran Ramanathan has been awarded the Crawford Prize,
considered the Nobel-level award for earth sciences, for his decades of research on pollution clouds and
chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs).
• While working at NASA, Ramanathan discovered that CFCs used in refrigerators and aerosols trap about
10,000 times more heat in the Earth's atmosphere than carbon dioxide, changing the understanding that
CO₂ was the only cause of global warming.
• His research also demonstrated that pollution clouds in the Indian Ocean Experiment were reducing the
impact of Indian monsoons and accelerating the melting of Himalayan glaciers.
• Ramanathan, born in Madurai, Tamil Nadu, is currently a Distinguished Professor Emeritus at Scripps
Institution of Oceanography, University of California.
• He will receive the Crawford Prize and a cash award of 8 million Swedish Krona in a ceremony in
Stockholm, Sweden, in May.
• Crawford Prize – Established: 1980; Awarded by: Royal Swedish Academy of Sciences; Recognizes
contributions to fields not covered by Nobel Prizes (e.g., earth sciences).
• Due to the harmful effects of CFCs, the Montreal Protocol was implemented to regulate their usage.
UNESCO Asia-Pacific Heritage Conservation Award 2025 for Our Lady of Grace Cathedral in Vasai
• The 475-year-old Our Lady of Grace Cathedral in Vasai, Maharashtra, has been awarded the UNESCO
Asia-Pacific Cultural Heritage Conservation Award 2025 for its preservation through traditional methods
and community involvement.
• This cathedral, built in the 16th century during Portuguese rule, is a significant historical site for Catholic
expansion in Maharashtra.
• The roof, bell tower, and other parts of the cathedral were restored at a cost of approximately ₹4.5 crores
due to the damage caused by monsoon effects and improper repairs.
• The restoration project was executed under the guidance of conservation architect Ainsley Lewis, with
local church members and officials collaborating. It serves as a model for community-based heritage
conservation.
• UNESCO – Established: 1945; Headquarters: Paris, France; Director-General: Khaled El Enany; Asia-
Pacific Cultural Heritage Conservation Awards – Established: 2000.
• Maharashtra Capital: Mumbai; Chief Minister: Devendra Fadnavis; Governor: Acharya Devvrat.

CONSTITUTION & POLITICS
Kerala Name Change & Free HPV Vaccination for Girls – Key Cabinet Decisions
• The Union Cabinet has approved the name change of Kerala to "Keralam", in line with the resolution
passed by the state government. This decision is in accordance with Article 3 of the Indian Constitution,
which allows changes in state names or boundaries.
• The Cabinet also approved a nationwide free HPV vaccination program for girls aged 14 years to prevent
cervical cancer. The Gardasil vaccine will be administered free of cost, with the Ministry of Health and
Family Welfare overseeing the program.
• HPV (Human Papillomavirus) is the leading cause of cervical cancer, and the World Health Organization
recommends the HPV vaccine for girls. Cervical cancer is one of the most common cancers among women
in India.
• The Union Cabinet is the highest executive decision-making body of the government, headed by the Prime
Minister of India, Narendra Modi.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 20 GVK Study Circle

• Kerala (Keralam) – Capital: Thiruvananthapuram; Governor: Arif Mohammed Khan; Chief Minister:
Pinarayi Vijayan.
• National Health Mission focuses on improving women's health and public health interventions, with the
HPV vaccine being a significant preventive measure.
National Commission for Women – 34th Foundation Day
• The National Commission for Women (NCW) celebrated its 34th Foundation Day in New Delhi,
reaffirming its commitment to women’s rights and empowerment.
• The NCW is a statutory body formed under the National Commission for Women Act, 1990. It works to
protect and promote the constitutional and legal rights of women, handle grievances, and advise on policy
matters.
• The commission was set up in 1992 based on recommendations regarding women’s status, and it is not a
constitutional body but a statutory institution.
• The NCW has the power to take suo-motu action on violations of women’s rights, call individuals for
investigations, and recommend amendments to laws related to women.
• NCW – Headquarters: New Delhi; Current Chairperson: Vijay Rahatkar; Ministry of Women and Child
Development.
National Commission for Scheduled Tribes (NCST) – 23rd Foundation Day
• The National Commission for Scheduled Tribes (NCST) celebrated its 23rd Foundation Day in New Delhi
in 2026, as a constitutional body formed under Article 338A of the Indian Constitution.
• The NCST is tasked with safeguarding and promoting the rights, welfare, and constitutional protection of
the tribal communities in India, as well as investigating grievances related to them.
• The Commission was established in 2004 under the 89th Constitutional Amendment Act after the
bifurcation of the earlier SC & ST Commission.
• The Commission comprises a Chairperson, Vice-Chairperson, and three members appointed by the
President of India.
• NCST Established: 19th February 2004; Headquarters: New Delhi; Current Chairperson: Antar Singh
Arya; Ministry of Tribal Affairs: Minister – Jual Oram.

Privileges Committee of Parliament
• The Privileges Committee and Ethics Committee of the Lok Sabha were not constituted for nearly two
years.
• The Privileges Committee functions as a semi-judicial body, safeguarding the special privileges of the
House and its members, rooted in British parliamentary traditions.
• Parliamentary privileges in India are based on constitutional provisions and traditions, as defined under
Articles 105 and 194.
• The committee’s primary goal is to examine violations that may undermine the dignity and authority of
the House.
• The committee has the authority to summon members or external individuals, record statements, and
request documents.
• Recommendations may include reprimands, suspensions, expulsions, or imprisonment during the session.
• Lok Sabha – Lower House of Parliament; Maximum Members: 552; Speaker: Om Birla; Established:
1952.
• Rajya Sabha – Upper House of Parliament; Maximum Members: 250; Chairman: Vice President of India,
C.P. Radhakrishnan.
No Confidence Motion Against Lok Sabha Speaker Om Birla
• On February 10, 2026, opposition parties moved a notice for a no-confidence motion against Lok Sabha
Speaker Om Birla, alleging partiality in the management of the House.
• The notice was submitted by Congress MP and Chief Whip K. Suresh, on behalf of Congress, Samajwadi
Party, and DMK, to the Lok Sabha Secretariat.
• The opposition raised objections over not allowing them to speak during the debate on the President's
address, and the suspension of 8 MPs.
• The no-confidence motion requires approval by a majority of members and a 14-day notice, as per Article
94(c) of the Indian Constitution.
• Attempts to remove a Speaker have been rare post-independence, with previous unsuccessful attempts in
1954, 1966, and 1987.

Official Secrets Act, 1923 - New Guidelines for Defense Personnel
• In the wake of the controversy over the unreleased memoirs of former Army Chief M.M. Naravane, the
Ministry of Defence is setting new guidelines for publishing books or memoirs by current and retired
defense personnel.
• The guidelines include provisions of the Official Secrets Act, 1923, which remain applicable even after
retirement.
• The Act, formulated during colonial times, protects official information, especially related to national
security and state interests.
• The Act aims to prevent espionage, unauthorized disclosure of sensitive information, and the improper
sharing of confidential data.
• The Ministry of Defence Establishment: 1947; Current Minister: Rajnath Singh.

• The BJP received ₹3,157.66 crore, while Indian National Congress received ₹298 crore (7.81%), and All

Revocation of President's Rule in Manipur
• The Ministry of Home Affairs issued a notification revoking the President's Rule in Manipur, following a
proclamation issued by the President of India.
• President Droupadi Murmu annulled the previous proclamation under Article 356(2) of the Constitution,

Supreme Court Recognizes Menstrual Health as a Fundamental Right
• In the case of Dr. Jay Thakur vs Union of India, the Supreme Court declared menstrual health a
fundamental right under Article 21 of the Constitution.
• The court clarified that menstrual hygiene, dignity, privacy, and bodily autonomy are included within the
right to live with dignity.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 23 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 24 ===
• The government was directed to provide sanitary pads and toilets as a constitutional responsibility, not as
an act of charity.
• The court also emphasized that the lack of proper menstrual management is an obstacle for girls, affecting
their right to equality and free and compulsory education under Articles 14 and 21A.
• Girls in grades 6 to 12 were directed to receive free biodegradable sanitary pads, and all schools should
have separate and accessible toilets for boys, girls, and differently-abled students.
• The judgment also sought awareness campaigns through Anganwadi workers and promoted the
installation of sanitary vending machines and incinerators in schools.
• UNESCO and UNICEF have recognized menstrual health as a human rights issue.
Supreme Court Guidelines on Solid Waste Management as Part of Right to Live in a Healthy
Environment
• The Supreme Court emphasized that environmental cleanliness and healthy surroundings are intrinsic to
the right to live under Article 21 of the Constitution.
• The court issued guidelines under the Solid Waste Management Rules, 2026, highlighting the need for
citizens and the government to act without delay to prevent the neglect of waste management.
• The court stressed that poor solid waste management harms public health and the economy, and to
maintain India’s global standing, environmental regulations must be enforced strictly.
• It noted the poor implementation of wet and dry waste separation and the continued use of dumping yards
in metro cities.
• The court also directed that local authorities, councils, and mayors take responsibility for spreading
awareness about environmental preservation.
• Solid Waste Management Rules – Notification Date: 2016; Ministry of Environment, Forest, and Climate
Change (MoEFCC) responsible for implementation.
• MoEFCC – Established: 1985; Headquarters: New Delhi; Minister: Bhupender Yadav.
Nyaya Setu – AI Chatbot for Free Legal Advice
• The Government of India launched "Nyaya Setu," an AI-based chatbot, on January 1, 2026, under the
Ministry of Law and Justice, to make legal services accessible to the general and middle-class population.
• The chatbot provides free primary legal advice on issues like marriage, divorce, family disputes, property
and land disputes, domestic violence, and criminal law matters.
• The chatbot is connected to the Tele-Law program, expanding digital legal services. Users can save the
number 72177 11814 on WhatsApp and send a "Hi" message to access the chatbot's services.
• Tele-Law is a government initiative to offer legal advice digitally.
• Article 39A of the Constitution supports free legal aid.

• The Chief Justice of India (CJI) handles complaints against Supreme Court judges, and the respective
Chief Justice of High Courts handles complaints against High Court judges.
• In-house procedures are used to investigate these complaints.
• Supreme Court – Established: 1950; Headquarters: New Delhi; Chief Justice: Justice Suryakant.

New Chief Justices for Andhra Pradesh and Madras High Courts
• The Supreme Court collegium has recommended Justice Lisa Gill for the next Chief Justice of Andhra

• Supreme Court Collegium – The system involves the Chief Justice and four senior-most judges
recommending appointments and transfers of judges.

Public Trust Doctrine – AP High Court Verdict on Tank Bunds
• The Andhra Pradesh High Court declared encroachments on Tank Bunds as a violation of the Public Trust
Doctrine, stating that water resources are public property, and the government acts as a trustee to protect
them for public use.
• The doctrine originated in Roman law and has been developed through English common law, applying to
natural resources like beaches, lakes, rivers, and wetlands.
• The Supreme Court of India officially recognized this doctrine in the M.C. Mehta vs. Kamal Nath (1997)
case.
• Article 21 of the Constitution includes the right to a healthy environment as part of the right to life.
First "Bharat Ratna Dr. Babasaheb Ambedkar Advocate Training and Research Center" Inaugurated
in India
• The Maharashtra Chief Minister Devendra Fadnavis and former Chief Justice of India Justice Bhushan R.
Gavai inaugurated India's first "Bharat Ratna Dr. Babasaheb Ambedkar Advocate Training and Research
Center" in Taloja, Navi Mumbai, set up by the Maharashtra and Goa Bar Council.
• The center aims to provide continuous training, skill development, and legal research, strengthening
professional standards in the legal field and offering modern legal knowledge.
• The center is named in honor of Dr. B.R. Ambedkar, the principal architect of the Indian Constitution and
a prominent legal expert.
• This center will play a key role in strengthening legal training infrastructure in Maharashtra.
• Maharashtra and Goa Bar Council – Established under the Advocates Act, 1961, they regulate the legal
profession under the supervision of the Bar Council of India.
• Maharashtra Capital: Mumbai; Chief Minister: Devendra Fadnavis; Governor: Bhagat Singh Koshyari.
• Dr. B.R. Ambedkar – Chairperson of the Drafting Committee of the Indian Constitution and India's first
Law Minister.
50th Anniversary of the Bonded Labour System (Abolition) Act, 1976
• The Bonded Labour System (Abolition) Act, 1976 completed 50 years in February 2026, which initially
came into force as an ordinance on October 25, 1975.
• The Act criminalized forced labour based on loans, aiming to eliminate modern slavery, though recent
incidents in Odisha indicate the challenges of timely rehabilitation of freed labourers.
• Despite the Act, bonded labour still exists in industries like brick kilns, poultry farms, and agriculture.
• The Act provides for the immediate abolition of bonded labour, and individuals subjected to it are declared
free.
• Article 23 of the Indian Constitution prohibits human trafficking and forced labour; Article 21 ensures the
right to live with dignity.

• Key Supreme Court judgments – Bandhua Mukti Morcha vs Union of India (1984), Neeraja Chaudhary

GEOGRAPHY AND ENVIRONMENT
Two New Ramsar Wetlands Added in India
• Union Minister for Environment, Forest and Climate Change Bhupender Yadav announced the addition
of two new wetlands to India’s Ramsar network Patna Bird Sanctuary in Etah district of Uttar Pradesh and
Chhari-Dhand wetland in Kutch district of Gujarat.
• With this addition, the total number of Ramsar sites in India increased to 98, compared to 26 in 2014,
registering a growth of more than 276%.
• Prime Minister Narendra Modi welcomed the expansion, stating that it reflects India’s commitment to
wetland conservation and biodiversity protection.
• In 2025, Siliserh Lake (Alwar, Rajasthan) and Kopra Reservoir (near Bilaspur, Chhattisgarh) were added
to the Ramsar list, raising the total to 96.
• In 2025, Gogabeel Lake (Bihar) and Gokul Reservoir (Buxar) and Udaypur Jheel (West Champaran) in
Bihar were also designated as Ramsar sites.
• On World Environment Day, Menar (Udaipur) and Khichan (Phalodi) wetlands in Rajasthan were added
to the Ramsar list.
• On World Wetlands Day (2 February), Sakkarakottai Bird Sanctuary and Therthangal Bird Sanctuary
(Tamil Nadu), Khecheopalri Wetland (Sikkim) and Udhwa Lake (Jharkhand) were recognized.
• The first Ramsar sites in India were Chilika Lake (Odisha) and Keoladeo National Park (Rajasthan).
• States with the highest Ramsar sites are Tamil Nadu (20) and Uttar Pradesh (11).
• The largest Ramsar site in India is the Sundarbans mangrove wetland (West Bengal), while the smallest is
Renuka Wetland (Himachal Pradesh).
• The Ramsar Convention was signed in 1971 in Ramsar, Iran, for conservation of wetlands of international
importance.
• India joined the convention on 1 February 1982 and currently has 98 Ramsar sites covering about 1.4
million hectares.
• India ranks 1st in Asia and 3rd globally in number of Ramsar sites, with 56 wetlands added after 2020.
• Wetlands are vital for biodiversity conservation, flood control, groundwater recharge, and carbon storage.
Dal Lake – Famous Freshwater Lake in Kashmir
• The Jammu and Kashmir government cancelled the ₹416.72 crore Dal Lake restoration plan and proposed
an in-situ conservation strategy, allowing residents around the lake to continue living there.
• Dal Lake is a famous urban freshwater lake and wetland ecosystem in Kashmir, known as the “Jewel in
the crown of Kashmir.”
• The lake supports tourism, fisheries, floating agriculture, and Kashmir’s cultural identity.
• It is located in Srinagar, the summer capital of Jammu and Kashmir, and is surrounded by Mughal gardens
such as Shalimar Bagh and Nishat Bagh.
• Dal Lake is connected to the Jhelum River system, receiving water from nearby streams and canals and
draining into the Jhelum River.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 26 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 27 ===
• Jammu and Kashmir is a Union Territory with Srinagar (summer capital) and Jammu (winter capital);
Lieutenant Governor – Manoj Sinha.
• The Jhelum River originates from Verinag in Jammu and Kashmir and is an important tributary of the
Indus River system.
Futala (Telangkhedi) Lake – Nagpur, Maharashtra
• Futala Lake (Telangkhedi Lake) is a man-made lake located in the western part of Nagpur, Maharashtra.
• It was constructed during the Bhonsle rule in the 18th century and is over 200 years old.
• The lake was built to supply water to nearby villages and agricultural lands.
• Spread across about 60 acres, it is one of the major lakes in Nagpur city.
• The lake is surrounded by forest on three sides, while the other side has a landscaped beach area.
• The ecosystem is threatened by untreated sewage inflow and increasing pollution.
• Urban lakes often face eutrophication due to untreated sewage, solid waste, and nutrient loading, which
damages water quality and biodiversity.
• The Nagpur Bhonsles were a Maratha dynasty that ruled the Nagpur region during the 18th–19th centuries.
• Maharashtra Capital: Mumbai; Chief Minister: Devendra Fadnavis; Governor: C. P. Radhakrishnan.
Sawalkot Mega Hydroelectric Project on Chenab River
• India has initiated the Sawalkot Mega Hydroelectric Project on the Chenab River following its exit from
the Indus Waters Treaty with Pakistan, amid the background of the Pahalgam terror attack.
• The Indus Waters Treaty (1960) between India and Pakistan, mediated by the World Bank, allocated Ravi,
Beas, and Sutlej rivers to India, and Indus, Jhelum, and Chenab rivers to Pakistan, with India having
limited rights on the western rivers.
• The Sawalkot project in Ramban district of Jammu and Kashmir was earlier delayed due to Pakistan’s
objections, but India is now proceeding with construction.
• The project is being implemented by the National Hydroelectric Power Corporation (NHPC).
• A tender worth ₹5,129 crore has been issued, and the selected firm must complete construction within
3,825 days.
• Once completed, the project will generate 1,856 MW of electricity, helping reduce power shortages in
Jammu and Kashmir and create local employment opportunities.
• The project will also strengthen India’s energy security, regional development, and strategic control over
water resources.
• Indus Waters Treaty – Signed: 19 September 1960; Countries: India and Pakistan; Mediator: World Bank;
Rivers: Indus, Jhelum, Chenab, Ravi, Beas, Sutlej.
• National Hydroelectric Power Corporation (NHPC) Established: 1975; Headquarters: Faridabad, Haryana;
Ministry: Ministry of Power.
• Sawalkot Project – River: Chenab; Location: Ramban district, Jammu and Kashmir; Capacity: 1,856 MW;
Type: Mega hydroelectric project.
• Jammu and Kashmir Capitals: Srinagar (summer), Jammu (winter); Lieutenant Governor: Manoj Sinha.
Shahpur Kandi Dam Project on Ravi River
• India is constructing the Shahpur Kandi Dam Project on the Ravi River in Pathankot district of Punjab,
near the Punjab–Jammu and Kashmir border, to utilize surplus Ravi waters that otherwise flow to Pakistan.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 27 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 28 ===
• The dam is located downstream of the Ranjit Sagar Dam and will use released water for hydropower
generation and irrigation.
• The project aims to ensure optimal utilization of the eastern rivers Ravi, Beas, and Sutlej allocated to India
under the Indus Waters Treaty (1960).
• Declared a national project in 2008, the cost is shared 80% by the Punjab Government and 20% by the
Central Government.
• Once completed, it will provide irrigation benefits to Punjab and Jammu and Kashmir, enhance
hydropower generation, and improve water resource utilization.
• Indus Waters Treaty – Signed in 1960 between India and Pakistan; Mediated by the World Bank.

Kerala Declares Tidal Flooding as a State-Specific Disaster
• The Kerala government has declared Tidal Flooding as a state-specific disaster for the first time; it is also
known as Sunny Day Flooding, King Tide Flooding, or Nuisance Flooding.
• This occurs when rising sea levels temporarily inundate coastal areas, influenced by strong winds, ocean
storms, and the gravitational pull of the Moon during full moon and new moon phases; it can occur twice
a day and is more intense during full moon and new moon.
• Along the Kerala coast, increasing Arabian Sea levels are causing temporary flooding in nearby areas, and
affected people will receive financial assistance through the State Disaster Response Fund (SDRF).
• The decision aims to strengthen climate-resilience and disaster preparedness in coastal regions.
• Kerala – Capital: Thiruvananthapuram; Chief Minister: Pinarayi Vijayan; Governor: Rajendra Arlekar;
Coastline length: about 590 km.
• State Disaster Response Fund (SDRF) is constituted under the Disaster Management Act, 2005; National
Disaster Management Authority (NDMA) – Established: 2005; Headquarters: New Delhi; Chairperson:
Prime Minister of India.
• The Arabian Sea, part of the northern Indian Ocean, lies along India’s western coast, and sea-level rise is
increasing tidal flooding incidents.
Lake Victoria Turns Green Due to Algal Bloom
• Lake Victoria’s water has turned green due to severe algal bloom, driven by nutrient runoff from
agriculture, polluted wastewater, and industrial effluents, leading to eutrophication.
• High levels of nitrogen and phosphorus accelerate algal growth, reducing dissolved oxygen in water,
affecting fish resources, biodiversity, and the livelihoods of local fishing communities.
• The situation is considered a major environmental warning sign in East Africa.
• Lake Victoria is the largest lake in Africa and the second-largest freshwater lake in the world by surface
area.
• It is mainly shared by Tanzania and Uganda, while also touching the border of Kenya; it is called Victoria
Nyanza in Kenya, Nalubaale in Uganda, and Ukerewe in Tanzania.
• The Equator passes through the lake, and it is one of the sources of the White Nile River.
• Tanzania – Capital: Dodoma; Currency: Tanzanian Shilling; President: Samia Suluhu Hassan.
• Uganda Capital: Kampala; Currency: Ugandan Shilling; President: Yoweri Museveni.
• Kenya Capital: Nairobi; Currency: Kenyan Shilling; President: William Ruto.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 28 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 29 ===
• Eutrophication is the process in which excess nutrients in water cause rapid algal growth and oxygen
depletion.
• Lake Superior is the largest freshwater lake in North America.
Takeshima / Dokdo Islands Dispute
• South Korea protested against “Takeshima Day” celebrations held in Japan’s Shimane Prefecture.
• The islands are called “Dokdo” by South Korea and “Takeshima” by Japan, located in the Sea of Japan
(called East Sea by South Korea).
• The island group consists of two main volcanic islets, Dongdo and Seodo, surrounded by 30–90 smaller
rocks and reefs.
• They lie about 87 km from Ulleungdo in South Korea and about 157 km from the Oki Islands of Japan.
• South Korea traces its claim back to the 6th-century Silla Kingdom, while Japan incorporated the islands
into Shimane Prefecture in 1905.
• After World War II, South Korea established Coast Guard control in 1954 and currently maintains de facto
administrative control.
• The surrounding waters are rich in fisheries due to ocean current convergence, and the area is believed to
contain gas hydrates (Fire Ice) reserves estimated at about 600 million tonnes.
• The potential value of these resources is estimated at about $10 billion annually.
• South Korea refuses to take the dispute to the International Court of Justice (ICJ), while Japan observes
Takeshima Day every year on February 22.
• Sea of Japan / East Sea is a marginal sea of the western Pacific Ocean, bordered by Japan, Russia, North
Korea, and South Korea.
• South Korea – Capital: Seoul; Currency: South Korean Won (KRW); President: Lee Jae-myung.
• Japan Capital: Tokyo; Currency: Japanese Yen (JPY); Prime Minister: Sanae Takaichi.
• International Court of Justice (ICJ) – Established: 1945; Headquarters: The Hague, Netherlands; Principal
judicial organ of the United Nations.
• Gas hydrates are considered a potential future energy resource, making the Dokdo/Takeshima dispute
geopolitically significant in East Asia.
Iran Temporarily Closes Strait of Hormuz Amid Nuclear Talks
• The Persian Gulf is shared by Iran, Iraq, Kuwait, Saudi Arabia, Bahrain, Qatar, the United Arab Emirates
(UAE), and Oman, and the Strait of Hormuz connects the Persian Gulf with the Gulf of Oman and the
Arabian Sea.
• In February 2026, during indirect nuclear negotiations between Iran and the United States in Geneva, Iran
announced the temporary closure of the Strait of Hormuz for security reasons and conducted military
exercises including missile launches.
• Due to heightened tensions in West Asia, the maritime route was closed for a few hours for security
purposes.
• The Strait of Hormuz is one of the most strategic sea routes in the world, through which about 20% of
global crude oil supply is transported.
• Major oil-exporting countries such as Saudi Arabia, Iraq, Kuwait, UAE, and Iran depend heavily on this
route.
• Iran’s Supreme Leader Ayatollah Ali Khamenei warned of a strong response if any attack occurs.

• Strait of Hormuz – Connects the Persian Gulf with the Gulf of Oman; narrowest width about 33 km; crucial

Strategic Importance of the Strait of Hormuz
• Amid Iran–US tensions, global attention has focused on the Strait of Hormuz due to its importance for
global energy supply.
• Oil tankers transporting crude from the Persian Gulf to global markets must pass through this route,
making it the lifeline of global energy trade.
• Around 20–25% of global oil trade and about 20% of natural gas trade passes through this strait.
• The strait lies between Iran’s coast and Oman’s Musandam Peninsula, with a minimum width of about 33
km.
• The United States deployed warships in the region, while Iran conducted military exercises near the strait.
• Closure of the strait could seriously impact India’s energy security, as over 50% of India’s crude oil
imports come from West Asia.
• India imports about 2 million barrels of oil per day from Iraq, Saudi Arabia, Kuwait, and UAE through
this route.
• Such disruption could also reduce natural gas imports by nearly 50%.
• Iraq Capital: Baghdad; Currency: Iraqi Dinar; President: Abdul Latif Rashid.
• China Capital: Beijing; Currency: Renminbi (Yuan); President: Xi Jinping.
• Kuwait Capital: Kuwait City; Currency: Kuwaiti Dinar; Emir: Mishal Al-Ahmad Al-Jaber Al-Sabah.
Chagos Islands – Location, Status and Geographical Features
• The United Kingdom and Mauritius are engaged in negotiations over the sovereignty of the Chagos
Islands, with opposition mentioned by US President Donald Trump.
• The Chagos Archipelago consists of over 60 tropical islands grouped into seven atolls.
• The islands are currently administered by the United Kingdom as the British Indian Ocean Territory
(BIOT), though Mauritius claims sovereignty.
• They are located in the Indian Ocean about 500 km south of the Maldives.
• Situated on the Chagos–Laccadive Ridge, the islands are coral atolls formed by volcanic activity of the
Réunion hotspot, with lagoons in the center.
• The Great Chagos Bank is considered the largest atoll structure in the world.
• British Indian Ocean Territory (BIOT) — Established: 1965; Administration: United Kingdom;
Administrative centre: Diego Garcia; hosts a US–UK military base.
• United Kingdom Capital: London; Currency: Pound Sterling; Head of State: King Charles III; Prime
Minister: Keir Starmer.
• Mauritius Capital: Port Louis; Currency: Mauritian Rupee; President: Dharambeer Gokhool; Prime
Minister: Navin Ramgoolam.
• The Chagos–Laccadive Ridge linking Lakshadweep, Maldives and Chagos is geopolitically important in
the Indian Ocean region, and the dispute has been discussed in the ICJ and UN General Assembly.

Vaan Island Artificial Reef Project – Socio-Environmental Impact Study
• A 10-year artificial reef project on Vaan Island has generated ₹61.67 crore in socio-environmental benefits,
more than double the inflation-adjusted project cost.
• The study was conducted by the Tamil Nadu Coastal Restoration Mission and partner institutions.
• Vaan Island, located in the Gulf of Mannar near the Thoothukudi island group in Tamil Nadu, previously
suffered from severe coastal erosion and land degradation.
• Artificial reefs strengthened coastal protection and helped restore marine biodiversity.
• The project is considered a cost-effective model for nature-based coastal restoration.
• Tamil Nadu Coastal Restoration Mission — Headquarters: Chennai; Established: 2021; Implemented by
the Tamil Nadu Department of Environment, Climate Change and Forests.
• Gulf of Mannar — Located between India and Sri Lanka; Declared a Biosphere Reserve in 1989; India’s
first marine biosphere reserve.
• Tamil Nadu — Capital: Chennai; Governor: R. N. Ravi; Chief Minister: M. K. Stalin.
• Artificial reefs are increasingly used as nature-based solutions for climate adaptation; India’s coastline
length is about 11,098 km.
Suav River Restored to River Status – NGT Orders
• The National Green Tribunal (NGT) directed the Uttar Pradesh government to reclassify the Suav River
from a “nallah” (drain) to a “river” and record it in revenue records and official gazette notifications.
• The Suav River, a tributary of the Rapti River in Balrampur district of Uttar Pradesh, was earlier
downgraded in classification.
• The tribunal warned that non-compliance with the NGT Act, 2010 may lead to penalties; the bench was
headed by Justice Prakash Shrivastava.
• The Balrampur District Collector was directed to update revenue records within three months.
• Construction in the Flood Plain Zones of Balrampur and Siddharthnagar districts must stop until the Active
Flood Zone is properly identified.
• The order strengthens river conservation and floodplain protection measures.
• National Green Tribunal (NGT) Established: 2010; Headquarters: New Delhi; Chairperson: Justice
Prakash Shrivastava.
• Uttar Pradesh Capital: Lucknow; Governor: Anandiben Patel; Chief Minister: Yogi Adityanath; most
populous state in India.
• Rapti River Origin: Lower Himalayas in Nepal; flows through Uttar Pradesh and joins the Ghaghara River.
South Coast Railway Zone Formation
• The Central Government plans to launch the new South Coast Railway Zone (SCoR) with headquarters at
Visakhapatnam from April 1.
• The new zone will include Vijayawada, Guntur and Guntakal divisions along with parts of the East Coast
Railway, as promised under the Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act, 2014.
• The Union Cabinet approved the proposal in February 2025, and the Detailed Project Report (DPR) with
financial and divisional details was submitted to the Railway Board.
• After reorganisation, South Central Railway (SCR) will be limited to Secunderabad, Hyderabad and
Nanded divisions.
• Some rail routes will be reorganized between divisions, including Raichur–Wadi, Vishnupuram–
Pagidipalli, Vishnupuram–Janpahad, and Motumarri–Kondapalli sections.

Volcán de Fuego Eruption in Guatemala
• Volcán de Fuego, one of the most active volcanoes in Guatemala, recently erupted with intense activity.
• The basaltic stratovolcano, whose name means “Volcano of Fire” in Spanish, frequently produces lava
flows, ash clouds, and explosive eruptions.
• It is located near the historic city Antigua, between Acatenango and Agua volcanoes, at an elevation of
about 3,763 meters above sea level.
• The volcano is part of the Central American Volcanic Arc, stretching from Guatemala to Panama.
• Guatemala lies along the Pacific Ring of Fire, a tectonically active zone prone to frequent earthquakes and
volcanic eruptions.
• Guatemala Capital: Guatemala City; Currency: Guatemalan Quetzal; President: Bernardo Arévalo.
• Antigua Guatemala A UNESCO World Heritage Site.
• The Pacific Ring of Fire contains about 75% of the world’s active volcanoes.
Piton de la Fournaise Volcano – Réunion Island
• Piton de la Fournaise volcano on Réunion Island erupted for the second time this year.
• It is a basaltic shield volcano located in the southeastern part of Réunion Island, a French territory in the
Indian Ocean.
• The volcano formed on the flank of the older Piton des Neiges volcano and produces basaltic and alkaline
lava eruptions.
• Frequent short eruptions, lava fountains and extensive lava flows are its key characteristics.
• About 500,000 years old, it is considered one of the most active volcanoes in the world.
• Réunion Island has been recognized as a UNESCO World Heritage Site since 2010.
• Shield volcano formed by low-viscosity lava creating broad gentle slopes.
• France Capital: Paris; Currency: Euro; President: Emmanuel Macron; Prime Minister: Sébastien Lecornu.
• UNESCO Established: 1945; Headquarters: Paris; Director-General: Khaled El-Enany.
Mount Kanlaon – Recent Eruption
• Mount Kanlaon, one of the most active stratovolcanoes in the Philippines, recently erupted releasing ash
clouds over Negros Island.
• Ashfall affected air quality and visibility, prompting safety warnings in nearby areas.
• The Philippine Institute of Volcanology and Seismology (PHIVOLCS) warned people not to enter the
permanent danger zone and issued aviation alerts.
• Mount Kanlaon is a stratovolcano in the Visayas region on Negros Island with an elevation of about 2,465
metres.
• It lies within the Pacific Ring of Fire, a tectonically active volcanic zone.

• The volcano is part of Mount Kanlaon Natural Park and has several pyroclastic cones and craters.
• Philippines Capital: Manila; Currency: Philippine Peso; President: Ferdinand “Bongbong” Marcos Jr.
• PHIVOLCS Established: 1982; Headquarters: Quezon City; monitors volcanoes, earthquakes and
tsunamis.

• On 5 February 2026, Defence Minister Rajnath Singh flagged off an Indian joint mountaineering

• Mount Aconcagua in the Andes of Argentina is the highest peak in South America (about 6,960 m) and

Pennaiyar River Water Dispute
• The Supreme Court directed the Union Government to constitute an Inter-State River Water Disputes
Tribunal within one month to resolve the Pennaiyar River water dispute between Tamil Nadu and
Karnataka.
• Hearing the case filed by Tamil Nadu under Article 131 of the Constitution, the Court referred to the Inter-
State River Water Disputes Act, 1956.
• The Pennaiyar River, called Thenpennai / Ponnaiyar in Tamil and Dakshina Pinakini in Kannada, is an
east-flowing inter-state river of South India.
• The river originates in the Nandi Hills region of Chikkaballapur district in Karnataka and flows through
the Eastern Ghats.
• Karnataka is the upstream state and Tamil Nadu the downstream state; the river is important for irrigation,
drinking water and industries, especially in Tamil Nadu.
• Inter-State River Water Disputes Act, 1956 provides the legal basis for tribunals to resolve interstate water
disputes.
• Article 131 gives the Supreme Court original jurisdiction in disputes between states and the Union.
• Article 262 provides for adjudication of interstate river water disputes through tribunals.
• Karnataka Capital: Bengaluru; Chief Minister: Siddaramaiah; Governor: Thawar Chand Gehlot.
• Tamil Nadu Capital: Chennai; Chief Minister: M. K. Stalin; Governor: R. N. Ravi.
Aravalli Range – Supreme Court Remarks on Jungle Safari Plan
• The Supreme Court refused permission to submit a revised Detailed Project Report for the proposed jungle
safari project in the Aravalli region.
• The bench comprising Justice Surya Kant, Justice Joymalya Bagchi and Justice Vipul M. Pancholi made
the observations.

• The Court stated that no activity will be allowed until experts clearly define the geographical and

• It acts as a natural barrier preventing the expansion of the Thar Desert and plays a key role in groundwater

Great Nicobar Project Gets NGT Approval
• With its 16 February 2026 order, the National Green Tribunal (NGT) dismissed fresh petitions and allowed
the ₹80,000-crore (about ₹81,000 crore) Great Nicobar mega infrastructure project to proceed.
• A 6-member bench led by Chairperson Justice Prakash Shrivastava noted the project’s strategic
importance and said the environmental clearance includes adequate safeguards.
• The tribunal also noted the review of the 2022 Environmental Clearance by a High-Powered Committee
and recorded the concerns raised by petitioners.
• Environmentalists have raised concerns about the 166 sq km development area and diversion of about 130
sq km of forest land.
• Major components include a transshipment port, integrated township, dual-use civil–military airport, and
gas plus solar power plant.
• The NGT said the project must proceed with strict compliance with all environmental conditions.
• Conservationists warn about possible impacts on biodiversity and indigenous tribes such as the Shompen
and Nicobarese.

Supreme Court Allows Road Construction Through Rajaji National Park
• The Supreme Court permitted road construction through Rajaji National Park but imposed a condition that

• The park is located in Uttarakhand between the Shivalik Range and the Indo-Gangetic Plains.
• It was formed by merging the Rajaji, Motichur and Chilla Wildlife Sanctuaries.
• The park is named after freedom fighter and India’s last Governor-General C. Rajagopalachari.
• Established in 1983, it was declared the Rajaji Tiger Reserve in 2015.

• On the 50th anniversary of Project Tiger, the Union Government formed four expert working groups to

• The groups will analyse past conservation strategies and recommend reforms to strengthen tiger

CCMB Study Warns of Long-Term Threat to Lion-Tailed Macaque
• Scientists from CSIR–Centre for Cellular and Molecular Biology (CCMB), Hyderabad warned of long-
term survival threats to the lion-tailed macaque based on a 40-year study in the Anamalai Hills of the
Western Ghats.
• The team led by Dr Govindaswamy Umapathy studied about 800 macaques in 37 groups across protected
forests and fragmented private estates.
• Groups in protected forests remain relatively stable, while those in fragmented habitats face higher long-
term extinction risk.
• Habitat fragmentation forces monkeys to move toward roads and settlements for food, increasing road
accidents and electrocution deaths.
• Researchers warned that without conservation measures in non-protected areas, the species’ long-term
survival is at risk.
• CCMB Established: 1977; Headquarters: Hyderabad, Telangana; Director: Dr Vinay K. Nandicoori; CSIR
national life sciences research laboratory.
• Lion-tailed Macaque Scientific name: Macaca silenus; Distribution: Western Ghats; IUCN Status:
Endangered.

• Species-wise count: Humpback dolphins 497, Irrawaddy dolphins 208, Bottlenose dolphins 55, Spinner
dolphins 3, Finless porpoise 2.

Diplocentrum papillosum – New Orchid Species Identified in Kerala
• Scientists discovered a new orchid species Diplocentrum papillosum in Kanthalloor and Marayur areas of
Idukki district in Kerala.
• It belongs to the Diplocentrum genus, a plant group endemic to the southern Western Ghats.
• The name papillosum refers to papilla-like structures on the flower surface.
• The plant grows on tree branches and rocks showing epiphytic and lithophytic characteristics and has
strong roots that withstand strong winds.
• Unbranched inflorescences, pink-white flowers and unique petal structure distinguish it from other
Diplocentrum species.
• The Western Ghats is a global biodiversity hotspot and was declared a UNESCO World Heritage Site in
2012.
• Orchids belong to the Orchidaceae family and many endemic orchid species occur in the Western Ghats.
• Kerala Capital: Thiruvananthapuram; Chief Minister: Pinarayi Vijayan; Governor: Rajendra Arlekar.
New Marine Worm Species Discovered on West Bengal Coast

• These species were identified along the West Bengal coast, adding new information to India’s marine

• The name solenotognatha comes from Greek words solenotos meaning channeled and gnatha meaning

Ministry: Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change.
• Polychaetes are segmented marine worms that play an important role in nutrient cycling and sediment

New Ant Species Discovered in Garbhanga Reserve Forest near Guwahati
• Scientists discovered a new ant species Polyrhachis garbhangensis in the Garbhanga Reserve Forest near
Guwahati in Assam.
• It belongs to the rare mucronata group of spiny ants in the Polyrhachis genus.
• The discovery highlights the rich biodiversity present even in forests close to urban areas.
• The species description was published on January 30 in the international journal Asian Myrmecology.
• The research was led by Ankita Sharma and supervised by Anindya Sinha of the National Institute of
Advanced Studies, Bengaluru.
• It is the third species of the mucronata group recorded in India, and among more than 70 Polyrhachis
species, 21 are recorded in Assam.
• Garbhanga Reserve Forest lies near Guwahati and forms part of the Assam–Meghalaya biodiversity
corridor and faces threats from urban expansion and forest fragmentation.
• Polyrhachis ants belong to the Formicidae family and are characterized by spiny bodies and arboreal or
ground nests, playing an important role in soil aeration and ecosystem balance.

New Dragonfly Species Liriothemis keralensis Identified in Kerala
• Researchers discovered a new dragonfly species Liriothemis keralensis, known as the “Slender
Bombardier”, in lowland coastal regions of Kerala.
• It belongs to the Liriothemis genus and was distinguished from Liriothemis asigastra found in Northeast
India.
• Key features include a slender abdomen, uniquely shaped tail and distinct reproductive structures.
• The dragonfly appears mainly during the monsoon season and disappears when rainfall declines.
• The discovery suggests that insect biodiversity in Kerala’s coastal ecosystems is still not fully studied.
• Dragonflies belong to the order Odonata and are sensitive to environmental changes, serving as indicators
of freshwater ecosystem health.
• The Liriothemis genus is distributed across South and Southeast Asia and inhabits wetlands, forest ponds
and seasonal rainwater pools.
• Kerala Capital: Thiruvananthapuram; Chief Minister: Pinarayi Vijayan; part of the Western Ghats
biodiversity hotspot.

New Army Ant Species Discovered in the Eastern Ghats of Andhra Pradesh

hunting.
• The Aenictus genus represents Old World army ants that prey mainly on other ants, termites and social
insects.

New Pistol Shrimp Species Alpheus madhusoodanai Identified in Kochi Backwaters
• Scientists discovered a new pistol shrimp species Alpheus madhusoodanai in the Kochi backwaters of
Kerala.
• It is also called snapping shrimp because it produces a loud snapping sound by rapidly closing its claw.
• The species belongs to the Alpheidae family and is the first alpheid shrimp recorded in the estuarine
ecosystem of the region.
• It shows burrowing behaviour in muddy substrates.
• Its burrowing helps release toxic gases trapped in mangrove sediments, improving soil quality and
strengthening coastal ecosystems.
• Estuary coastal water body where river water mixes with seawater.
• Mangroves salt-tolerant coastal forests important for shoreline protection and carbon storage.
