New Tiny Reef Crab Species Galathea balasubramaniani Found in Lakshadweep
• Scientists discovered a new tiny reef crab species Galathea balasubramaniani in coral reefs near Agatti
Island in Lakshadweep.
• It belongs to the Galathea genus of anomuran marine crabs, commonly called squat lobsters.
• The species measures only about 3 millimetres and is among the smallest reef crabs recorded in the region.
• It resembles miniature lobsters and hides in crevices of coral rocks.
• It differs from Galathea mauritiana in leg structure, body features and genetic characteristics.
• Lakshadweep Capital: Kavaratti; Administrator: Praful Khoda Patel; Union Territory in the Arabian Sea.

New Cobra Lily Species Arisaema siahaense Discovered in Mizoram
• Researchers discovered a new cobra lily species Arisaema siahaense in Siaha district of Mizoram.
• It is an evergreen medicinal herb that grows up to about 1.08 metres (around 3.5 feet).

• The plant grows from a rounded underground tuber and has leaves divided into three parts.
• The discovery adds a new record to plant taxonomy and biodiversity studies of Northeast India.

Cyclone Gezani in Madagascar – Damage and Relief Efforts
• Cyclone Gezani struck Madagascar in February 2026 bringing heavy rains, floods and strong winds
causing widespread damage.
• Initial reports recorded at least 20 deaths, later updated to at least 36 fatalities with hundreds injured.
• The cyclone made landfall on the eastern coast and severely affected infrastructure and houses in the port
city of Toamasina, disrupting services such as electricity.
• Around 17,980 houses were completely destroyed and more than 37,000 houses were damaged, affecting
over 250,000 people.
• Wind speeds exceeded 195 km per hour, increasing the risk of floods and landslides as the storm moved
inland.
• Madagascar Capital: Antananarivo; Currency: Malagasy Ariary; President: Andry Rajoelina; Prime
Minister: Christian Ntsay.
SCHEMES IN NEWS
PM-RAHAT (Road Accident Victim Hospitalization and Assured Treatment) Scheme
• Prime Minister Narendra Modi approved the PM-RAHAT scheme to provide cashless treatment for road
accident victims across India.
• Each eligible victim can receive treatment up to ₹1.5 lakh for a period of 7 days from the date of the
accident.
• Objective: provide immediate medical assistance and reduce fatalities during the “Golden Hour”.
• Through integration with ERSS-112, victims or Good Samaritans can call 112 to access the nearest
hospital and ambulance services.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 41 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 42 ===
• Stabilisation treatment is provided up to 24 hours for non-critical cases and 48 hours for critical cases,
with digital police verification support.
• Hospitals will be reimbursed through the Motor Vehicle Accident Fund.
• A grievance redressal officer appointed by the District Road Safety Committee will handle complaints.
• Launch: 2026; Implementing Ministry: Ministry of Road Transport and Highways.
• Motor Vehicle Accident Fund — Legal basis: Motor Vehicles Act, 1988 (Section 164B).
• Golden Hour Scheme — Legal basis: Motor Vehicles Act, 1988 (Section 162).
Lead Bank Scheme Reforms – 2026
• The Reserve Bank of India launched the Lead Bank Scheme in 1969 based on the recommendations of the
Nariman Committee to coordinate district-level development.
• Its main objective is to ensure coordination among banks, government departments and development
agencies.
• The scheme helps increase credit flow to priority sectors and promote financial inclusion.
• On February 13, 2026, RBI released a draft proposing amendments to the scheme’s guidelines.
• The revisions aim to improve the effectiveness of district-level financial development planning and
implementation.
• Reserve Bank of India — Established: 1935; Headquarters: Mumbai; Governor: Sanjay Malhotra.
RAMP Programme – Raising and Accelerating MSME Performance
• The progress of the RAMP programme supported by the World Bank was reviewed during the 5th National
MSME Council meeting organised by the Ministry of MSME.
• The programme was launched in 2022 and will run from 2022-23 to 2026-27.
• Its main objective is to improve MSMEs’ access to markets and credit.
• It also aims to strengthen coordination and institutional capacity between the central and state
governments.
• The programme promotes timely payments to MSMEs and supports sustainable growth through greening
initiatives.
• The National MSME Council acts as the administrative and operational body, providing grants to states

SAHI and BODH Initiatives for Responsible Health AI in India
• Union Health and Family Welfare Minister Jagat Prakash Nadda launched two national Health-AI
initiatives, SAHI and BODH, on 17 February 2026 at the India AI Impact Summit 2026 held at Bharat
Mandapam, New Delhi.
• SAHI (Strategy for Artificial Intelligence in Healthcare for India) is designed as a national governance
framework, policy guideline and long-term roadmap for responsible adoption of AI in healthcare.
• BODH (Benchmarking Open Data Platform for Health AI) is a national platform for benchmarking and
validating Health-AI solutions before large-scale deployment.
• The nodal ministry for both initiatives is the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare (MoHFW).
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 42 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 43 ===
• These initiatives act as “guardrails” to ensure safe, ethical, transparent and accountable use of AI in
healthcare systems.
• Responsible Health-AI implementation is linked with digital health infrastructure initiatives such as
Digital India (2015), National Health Policy (2017) and Ayushman Bharat Digital Mission (ABDM–
2020).
• SAHI provides a common governance framework for Union and State governments and the private sector
to ensure ethical, transparent and people-centric AI usage.
• Key focus areas include patient data protection and algorithmic accountability.
• BODH was developed jointly by IIT Kanpur and the National Health Authority (NHA).
• It serves as a national benchmarking platform to test AI models on anonymised real-world health datasets
for performance, bias and scalability across large populations.
• It ensures AI tools used by doctors are validated with real-world standards before large-scale deployment.
• Launch: 17 February 2026; Nodal Ministry: Ministry of Health and Family Welfare.
• National Health Authority (NHA) — Established: 2019; Headquarters: New Delhi; CEO: Sunil Kumar
Barnwal.
• Digital India — Launched: 2015; National Health Policy — 2017; Ayushman Bharat Digital Mission —
Launched: 15 August 2020.

Urban Challenge Fund of ₹1 Lakh Crore Approved by Union Cabinet
• The Union Cabinet approved the ₹1 lakh crore Urban Challenge Fund to develop cities as engines of
economic growth.
• Funding pattern: Centre 25%, States 25%, and the remaining 50% to be mobilised from the market.
• The initiative is expected to attract around ₹4 lakh crore investments in urban development over the next
five years.
• It marks a shift from grant-based funding to a market-oriented and results-focused urban development
model.
• Projects will be selected through a competitive “challenge mode”, with fund release linked to reforms,
milestones and outcomes.
• Monitoring of projects and reforms will be carried out through a paperless digital portal of the Ministry of
Housing and Urban Affairs (MoHUA).
• Key project verticals include “Cities as Growth Hubs”, “Creative Redevelopment of Cities”, and “Water
and Sanitation”.
• Operational period: FY 2025–26 to FY 2030–31, with possible extension up to FY 2033–34.

Soil Health Card Scheme
• More than 25 crore Soil Health Cards have been distributed to farmers across India.
• The Government of India launched the scheme in 2015 to address nutrient deficiencies in soil.
• The main objective is to promote balanced use of nutrients in agriculture.
• Over 93,000 farmer training programmes and more than 6 lakh field demonstrations have been conducted.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 45 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 46 ===
• Awareness campaigns have been organised nationwide to educate farmers.
• Soil Health Cards are issued to farmers once every two years.
• They provide information on soil nutrients, pH value, organic carbon and other physical–chemical
properties.
• The cards recommend appropriate fertilizer use, bio-fertilizers and soil amendments for different crops.
• The scheme helps farmers take informed decisions for long-term soil health management.
• Implementing Ministry: Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare; associated with the National
Mission for Sustainable Agriculture.

• The Pension Fund Regulatory and Development Authority launched the NPS Swasthya Pension Scheme
as a pilot project.
• It is implemented under the PFRDA Regulatory Sandbox framework.
• The scheme links pension savings under the National Pension System with healthcare expenditure.
• It allows pension savings to support outpatient and hospital treatment costs.
• Implemented initially as a proof of concept to test technical and regulatory feasibility before full rollout.
• The aim is to integrate long-term retirement planning with health security and reduce out-of-pocket
medical expenses.
• PFRDA Established: 2003; Legal basis: PFRDA Act, 2013; Headquarters: New Delhi; Chairperson:
Sivasubramanian Ramann; under the Ministry of Finance.

2009; defined contribution pension system.
• Regulatory Sandbox mechanism to test new financial products in a controlled regulatory environment.

Bharat Taxi – India’s First Cooperative Ride-Hailing Platform
• Union Cooperation Minister Amit Shah launched Bharat Taxi on 5 February 2026 in New Delhi as India’s
first cooperative-based ride-hailing platform.
• The platform was launched by the Ministry of Cooperation to strengthen the cooperative sector and
promote people-centric transport services.
• Drivers are called “Sarathis” and are given a central role in ownership, management and profit sharing.
• It operates on a cooperative model with zero commission and no surge pricing, distributing profits directly
to drivers.
• Social security benefits such as health insurance, accident insurance and retirement savings schemes are
provided for drivers’ welfare.
• The “Bike Didi” initiative promotes women’s participation, with more than 150 women drivers joining so
far.
• Ministry of Cooperation Established: 2021; Minister: Amit Shah; objective: strengthening the cooperative
movement and inclusive economic development.

• Airports Authority of India signed an MoU with Bharat Taxi to provide regulated and passenger-friendly
taxi services at airports.
• The partnership aims to offer transparent and reliable last-mile transport services.
• Bharat Taxi operates on a cooperative driver-ownership model with fixed fares and no surge charges.
• It has partnerships with organisations such as Delhi Traffic Police, Delhi Metro Rail Corporation (DMRC)
and State Bank of India.
• The initiative aims to standardise regulated taxi services across airports.

NeophyteID – AI App to Identify Invasive Plants in Kerala
• NeophyteID is an AI-based mobile application launched by the Kerala Chief Minister to identify invasive
plant species in the state.
• The app was developed by the Malabar Botanical Garden and Institute for Plant Sciences.
• Users can identify plants using image recognition technology by uploading photos taken through a mobile
camera or from the gallery.
• Geospatial tracking records the exact location of plants, supporting long-term ecological monitoring.
• The platform encourages citizen participation to detect and control the spread of invasive plant species at
an early stage.

Sabhasaar Initiative – AI Tool for Summarising Gram Sabha Meetings
• Sabhasaar is an AI platform that converts audio recordings of Gram Sabha and Panchayat meetings into
voice-to-text transcripts and automatically generates meeting minutes.
• Launched on 14 August 2025, the initiative was adopted by more than 1.11 lakh Gram Panchayats across
India by January 2026.
• It transforms meeting audio into searchable and standardised reports, reducing manual effort and
improving transparency.
• The tool helps monitor implementation of Gram Sabha resolutions, enhance accountability of local bodies
and create permanent digital records.

NAMASTE Scheme – Enumeration of Waste Collection Workers
• Under the National Action for Mechanised Sanitation Ecosystem (NAMASTE) scheme, the Government
of India released enumeration data of 1.52 lakh waste collection workers.
• The enumeration was conducted through a dedicated mobile application launched in 2025.
• Around 84.5% of the workers belong to Scheduled Castes, Scheduled Tribes and Other Backward Classes,
while 10.7% belong to the general category (mainly in Delhi and Goa).
• The scheme is implemented jointly by the Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment and the Ministry
of Housing and Urban Affairs.
• It aims to promote mechanised sanitation and ensure safety, dignity and livelihood protection for sanitation
workers.
• NAMASTE replaced the Self Employment Scheme for Rehabilitation of Manual Scavengers (SRMS).
• It is implemented by the National Safai Karamcharis Finance and Development Corporation.
• The enumeration helps in better targeting of welfare schemes, skill development and financial assistance
for sanitation workers.
• NAMASTE — Launched: 2022; Central Sector Scheme; objective: elimination of manual scavenging and
promotion of mechanised sanitation.
• National Safai Karamcharis Finance and Development Corporation — Established: 1997; Headquarters:
New Delhi; provides financial support and rehabilitation for sanitation workers.
Pradhan Mantri Bhartiya Janaushadhi Pariyojana (PMBJP)
• The Government of India launched the Pradhan Mantri Bhartiya Janaushadhi Pariyojana to provide quality
generic medicines at affordable prices.
• Under the scheme, Janaushadhi Kendras are established across the country to reduce healthcare
expenditure for citizens.
• Medicines are available at prices 50–80% lower than branded drugs.
• Currently around 2,110 medicines and 315 surgical items, medical consumables and devices are available.
• These include medicines for heart disease, cancer, diabetes, infections, allergies, gastrointestinal disorders
and nutritional products.
• The government aims to establish 20,000 Janaushadhi Kendras across India by 31 March 2026.
• The scheme strengthens affordable healthcare access for the general public.
• PMBJP Launched: 2008 (revamped in 2015); Implementing agency: Pharmaceuticals and Medical
Devices Bureau of India; Central Sector health scheme.
• Pharmaceuticals and Medical Devices Bureau of India — Established: 2010; under the Ministry of
Chemicals and Fertilizers; responsible for operating Janaushadhi Kendras.
LF Mass Drug Administration (MDA) Campaign
• Union Health Minister JP Nadda launched the national annual MDA campaign in February 2026 via video
conference to eliminate Lymphatic Filariasis (LF)/Elephantiasis, with India aiming to eliminate LF as a
public health problem by 2027.
• From 2026 the programme was unified into one MDA round per year instead of two.
• The goal is to reduce the microfilaria (Mf) rate below 1% in endemic areas to stop disease transmission.
• It forms part of India’s Enhanced Five-Pronged Strategy: mission mode MDA, case detection & morbidity
management, vector control, inter-sectoral coordination and digital surveillance.
• Currently 124 districts in 12 states are covered: 55 double-drug (DA) districts and 69 triple-drug (IDA)
districts across 719 blocks.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 49 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 50 ===
• Drug regimens: DA – DEC + Albendazole; IDA – Ivermectin + DEC + Albendazole.
• LF is a mosquito-borne parasitic disease mainly caused by Wuchereria bancrofti, transmitted by Culex
quinquefasciatus.
• Ministry of Health and Family Welfare — Headquarters: Nirman Bhavan, New Delhi; Minister: JP Nadda.
SANKALP Scheme (Skill Acquisition and Knowledge Awareness for Livelihood Promotion)
• The Public Accounts Committee criticised delays, poor fund utilisation and weak monitoring in the
implementation of the SANKALP scheme.
• SANKALP stands for Skill Acquisition and Knowledge Awareness for Livelihood Promotion and aims to
improve the quality of short-term skill training in India.
• The scheme was launched on 19 January 2018, initially planned until March 2023 and later extended.
• Implementing ministry: Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship (MSDE).
• It is a World Bank-assisted centrally sponsored scheme implemented with state governments and industry
partners.
• The objective is to strengthen institutional mechanisms for skill development at national, state and district
levels and improve coordination among skill programmes.
• It also focuses on better monitoring, evaluation, quality assurance and increased industry participation.
• Special emphasis is given to inclusion of women, SCs, STs, minorities and other backward classes.
• MSDE — Established: 2014; Headquarters: New Delhi; Minister: Jayant Chaudhary.
• World Bank — Established: 1944; Headquarters: Washington DC; President: Ajay Banga.
• Public Accounts Committee — Established: 1921; Parliamentary financial oversight committee.
Vanajeevi Didi Initiative in Palamau Tiger Reserve
• Palamau Tiger Reserve in Jharkhand launched the “Vanajeevi Didi” initiative as a community-based
wildlife conservation programme.
• The programme was launched on 17 February 2026 in the Garu Range and is implemented by the PTR
South Division.
• From 17 interior villages such as Henar, Surkumi, Hasuwa, Armu, Kotam and Pandra, 18 educated women
from each village were selected.
• Preference was given to women pursuing graduation/post-graduation or those with experience in
environmental activities.
• Their main responsibility is to discourage illegal activities such as poaching and forest destruction.
• Each member receives an incentive of ₹3,000 per month; the programme will initially run for two months
before review.
• The initiative is inspired by Sakhi Mandal and Para-Legal Volunteer models.
• Palamau Tiger Reserve is part of Project Tiger (Project Tiger launched on 1 April 1973).

the National Tiger Conservation Authority.

• The Swavalambini pilot programme was launched in February 2025 by the Ministry of Skill Development
and Entrepreneurship (MSDE) in collaboration with the NITI Aayog Women Entrepreneurship Platform

• It is implemented in six HEIs/Universities across Assam, Meghalaya, Mizoram, Uttar Pradesh and

covering finance, market linkages, legal compliance and networking.
• A 21-week mentorship and hand-holding phase helps convert ideas into startups.
• A 5-day Faculty Development Programme (FDP) is also conducted for faculty members.
• As of 29 January 2026 — FDP: 75 participants; EAP: 1,110 participants; EDP: 302 participants trained.
• MSDE allocated ₹40,46,016 and released ₹10,11,504 for the programme; it is currently in the pilot stage.

National Monetisation Pipeline 2.0 (NMP 2.0)
• The Union Finance Minister launched the National Monetisation Pipeline 2.0 (NMP 2.0), the second phase
of the asset monetisation programme.
• It provides a medium-term roadmap to unlock value from operational brownfield public infrastructure
assets.
• The objective is to mobilise new capital resources for infrastructure development and promote asset
recycling without increasing the fiscal deficit.
• It encourages greater private sector participation in infrastructure and improves transparency in
monetisation opportunities.
• The initiative supports the implementation of the National Infrastructure Pipeline (NIP).
• Asset Monetisation refers to leasing revenue rights of public assets to the private sector for a fixed period
while ownership remains with the government.
• First National Monetisation Pipeline — Launched: 2021.
• Major sectors covered include roads, railways, power transmission, airports and ports.
• NITI Aayog — Established: 1 January 2015; Headquarters: New Delhi; Chairperson: Narendra Modi;
Vice-Chairperson: Suman Bery.
• Ministry of Finance — Headquarters: New Delhi; Finance Minister: Nirmala Sitharaman.

MANAV Vision – India’s Framework for Human-Centric Artificial Intelligence Governance
• Prime Minister Narendra Modi announced the MANAV Vision (Modi Artificial Intelligence – Nation
Aligned Vision) at the India AI Impact Summit 2026 as India’s approach to human-centric AI governance.
• It focuses on placing human values, ethics, trust, inclusiveness and accountability at the centre of AI
development while balancing technological innovation with constitutional values and legal safeguards.
• Under the concept of “AI for All”, it aims to expand AI accessibility in governance, education, healthcare
and agriculture while emphasizing data sovereignty and privacy protection.
• Objective — to position India as a global leader in responsible AI development and deployment.

Kerala Nativity Card Bill – Official Identity for State Services
• The Kerala State Cabinet approved the Kerala Nativity Card Bill to introduce a Nativity Card as an official
identity document for accessing state services, benefits and reservations.
• The bill is expected to be introduced in the Kerala Legislative Assembly session scheduled on 23 February
2026.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 53 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 54 ===
• A “native” refers to a person born in Kerala who has not acquired foreign citizenship, or someone with
ancestors born in Kerala who has not renounced Indian citizenship.
• Individuals who have obtained foreign citizenship will not be eligible, and the card becomes invalid if
such citizenship is acquired later.
• The Tahsildar will act as the issuing authority, village offices will maintain official registers, and appeals
can be made before the District Collector.
• Objective to simplify nativity verification for administrative purposes.
• Relevant constitutional provisions include Articles 5–11 (Citizenship), Articles 14, 15, 16, 19 and 371.
• Related laws include the Citizenship Act 1955, Registration of Births and Deaths Act 1969 and the Indian
Evidence Act 1872.
• Important Supreme Court judgments include Dr. Pradeep Jain vs Union of India (1984), Kailash Chand
Sharma vs State of Rajasthan (2002), Sunanda Reddy vs State of Andhra Pradesh (1995) and AVS
Narasimha Rao vs State of Andhra Pradesh (1969).

• The India AI Impact Summit 2026 was held in New Delhi from 16–20 February 2026.

AI Impact Summit 2026 Concludes with New Delhi Declaration
• The AI Impact Summit 2026 held in New Delhi concluded with the adoption of the New Delhi Declaration
on AI Impact, supported by India along with 88 countries and international organisations.
• The declaration reflects global consensus on responsible and inclusive AI use and provides guiding
principles for economic growth and social welfare.
• Based on the principle “Sarvajan Hitaya, Sarvajan Sukhaya”, it emphasises that the benefits of AI should
reach all sections of society.
• Inspired by the concept of “Vasudhaiva Kutumbakam”, it calls for stronger international cooperation in
developing AI for the welfare of humanity.
• It encourages collaboration among governments, industries, academic institutions and civil society in AI
research, governance and implementation.
• The declaration stresses the development of transparent and trustworthy AI frameworks while respecting
the sovereignty of nations.
• The summit further strengthened India’s role in shaping global AI governance.
• AI Impact Summit 2026 Organised by: Government of India; Venue: New Delhi; Focus: global AI
governance and inclusive development.

Andhra Pradesh Budget 2026–27 (₹3,32,205 Crore)
• Andhra Pradesh Finance Minister Payyavula Keshav presented the ₹3,32,205 crore budget for the financial
year 2026–27.
• The budget aims to balance state development and public welfare.
• Total budget size ₹3,32,205 crore; Revenue expenditure ₹2,56,143 crore; Capital expenditure ₹53,915
crore.
• Revenue deficit ₹22,002 crore; Fiscal deficit ₹75,868 crore.
• ₹6,000 crore allocated for the development of Amaravati capital city.
• Allocations include ₹13,934 crore for the power sector, ₹13,546 crore for roads, ports and airports, and
₹3,161 crore for industries.
• ₹5,451 crore allocated for housing, ₹4,000 crore for NTR Vaidya Seva, ₹4,000 crore for Jal Jeevan Mission
and ₹1,037 crore for Swachh Bharat Mission.
• ₹28,000 crore allocated for the Visakha Economic Region covering 10 districts, while 9 districts were
included under the Amaravati Economic Region.
• ₹30,000 crore announced for the Rayalaseema Global Horticulture Hub.
• Decision taken to establish special cyber crime police stations in every district.
• Allocations include ₹32,308 crore for School Education, ₹22,942 crore for Panchayati Raj, and ₹13,598
crore for agriculture and allied sectors.
• ₹6,660 crore allocated for the Annadata Sukhibhava scheme and ₹6,105 crore for the Polavaram Project.
• ₹9,165 crore allocated for the Home Department, ₹14,539 crore for Municipal Administration and ₹18,224
crore for the Water Resources Department.
• ₹300 crore allocated for hostels and gurukuls, ₹3,836 crore for student scholarships and ₹2,161 crore for
the Mid-Day Meal scheme.

• Andhra Pradesh Capital: Amaravati; Chief Minister: N. Chandrababu Naidu; Governor: S. Abdul Nazeer;
Finance Minister: Payyavula Keshav.
• Revenue expenditure refers to day-to-day operational expenses, while capital expenditure refers to
spending that creates assets such as infrastructure.
• Revenue deficit = Revenue expenditure − Revenue receipts; Fiscal deficit = Total expenditure − (Total
receipts excluding borrowings).

Performance-based Constituency Grading in Andhra Pradesh
• The Andhra Pradesh government introduced a performance-based grading system for all 175 Assembly
constituencies based on data collected up to January.
• Vijayawada West constituency ranked first in the state, while Guntur West ranked last.
• Overall results — 21 constituencies received A+ grade, 153 constituencies received A– grade and only
one constituency received B– grade; these details were revealed during a ministers–secretaries meeting at
the State Secretariat.
• Grading criteria: above 90% score — A+; 75–89% — A–; 50–74% — B–.
• For the first time in India, a Constituency Vision Action Plan (CVAP) system was introduced with teams
including an MLA, special officer, knowledge partner, young professional and village/ward secretariat
staff.
• A total of 685 indicators were used to assess departmental performance, grievance redressal, P4
programmes and development works.
• Major A+ constituencies include Vijayawada West (93), Gajuwaka (92), Kakinada City (92), Vijayawada
East (91), Hindupur (91), Penugonda (91), Visakhapatnam West (91), Madugula (91), Pithapuram (91),
Ramachandrapuram (91), Jaggampeta (91) and Gopalapuram (SC) (91).
• Lowest A– constituencies include Kondapi (79), Kandukur (78), Kavali (78) and Ongole (77); B– grade
constituency Guntur West (73).
• Andhra Pradesh Capital: Amaravati; Chief Minister: N. Chandrababu Naidu; Assembly constituencies:
175.
• Constituency Vision Action Plan (CVAP) is a performance-based governance model aimed at achieving
results-oriented development at the constituency level.

Agricultural Loans in Andhra Pradesh Increased by 77% in Two Years
• Agricultural credit in Andhra Pradesh increased by 77% in the last two years, indicating significant growth
in loans provided to farmers.
• In 2023, banks sanctioned agricultural loans worth ₹1.27 lakh crore, which increased to ₹2.25 lakh crore
by 2025.
• During the same period, the national average growth of agricultural credit was about 41%, which is lower
than Andhra Pradesh.
• According to Union Minister of State for Finance Pankaj Chaudhary, Andhra Pradesh has emerged as one
of the leading states in agricultural credit growth.
• Andhra Pradesh Capital: Amaravati; Chief Minister: N. Chandrababu Naidu; agriculture is a key sector in
the state economy.
• Ministry of Finance (India) responsible for financial policy, banking regulation, taxation and public
finance management.
AP Chief Minister Objects to Almatti Dam Height Increase Proposal
• Andhra Pradesh Chief Minister N. Chandrababu Naidu objected to the proposal to increase the height of
the Almatti Dam on the Krishna River in Karnataka and informed the central government.
• He stated that increasing the dam height could affect water availability for downstream areas of Andhra
Pradesh.
• Almatti Dam is the main reservoir of the Upper Krishna Irrigation Project (UKIP) used for irrigation,
power generation and flood control.
• Dam details approximately 52.5 metres height, about 3.5 km length and annual power generation of around
713 million units.
• The Krishna River flows through Maharashtra, Karnataka, Telangana and Andhra Pradesh and is a major
peninsular river.
• Inter-state river water disputes are resolved through tribunals under the Inter-State River Water Disputes
Act, 1956.
• Andhra Pradesh Capital: Amaravati; Chief Minister: N. Chandrababu Naidu; Governor: S. Abdul Nazeer.
• Karnataka Capital: Bengaluru; Chief Minister: Siddaramaiah; Governor: Thaawarchand Gehlot.
India’s Largest Lithium-Ion Battery Gigafactory in Andhra Pradesh
• Waaree Energies announced plans to establish India’s largest integrated lithium-ion battery gigafactory in
Andhra Pradesh.
• The greenfield facility will be set up at Rambilli in Anakapalli district.
• The project involves an investment of about ₹8,175 crore (around USD 890 million) with a production
capacity of about 16 GWh.
• The project received in-principle approval from the State Investment Promotion Board (SIPB) chaired by
Chief Minister N. Chandrababu Naidu.
• The facility will cover the complete production chain from lithium-ion cell manufacturing to battery packs
and large-scale Battery Energy Storage Systems.
• The project will support renewable energy integration, electric vehicles and grid-scale energy storage
while strengthening domestic battery manufacturing under Atmanirbhar Bharat.
• Waaree Energies Limited — Established: 1990; Headquarters: Mumbai; Chairman & Managing Director:
Hitesh Doshi.

India’s First Indigenous Green Urea Plant in Andhra Pradesh
• In February 2026, NTPC Green Energy Limited and Asago Industries Pvt Ltd signed a memorandum of
understanding to set up India’s first large-scale indigenous green urea manufacturing facility.
• The project will be located at the Poodimadaka Green Hydrogen Hub near Visakhapatnam.
• NTPC Green Energy will supply green ammonia, carbon dioxide, renewable electricity, water, and other
utilities required for a 1,000-tonne per day green urea plant.
• Production using renewable hydrogen and green ammonia instead of traditional fossil-fuel-based urea will
significantly reduce carbon emissions.
• The project aims to reduce India’s urea imports by 8–10 million tonnes out of the 35–40 million tonnes
consumed annually.
• The green urea plant will be part of the Poodimadaka Green Hydrogen Hub, producing green ammonia,
green methanol, sustainable aviation fuel, and green urea.
• NTPC Green Energy Limited subsidiary of NTPC Limited focused on green hydrogen, green ammonia,
and renewable energy projects.
• NTPC Limited Established: 1975; Headquarters: New Delhi; Chairman & MD: Gurdeep Singh; Central
Public Sector Enterprise.
• Green urea projects are key to India’s fertilizer self-sufficiency, environmental protection, and 2070 net-
zero targets.

Computer Didi – Didi Ka Dukan Scheme
• The central government launched “Computer Didi – Didi Ka Dukan” to provide digital employment and
financial self-reliance for rural women.
• Implemented as a pilot in five states with support from UNICEF and state governments, Guntur district
(Tadikonda Mandal) in Andhra Pradesh was selected for the pilot.
• The scheme enables rural women to provide digital services and operate retail centers at the village level.
• Women aged 18–29 with degrees, engineering, or higher education are eligible for selection.
• During the pilot, 2,500 women will be trained as “Lakpati Didis”; if successful, the scheme aims to
establish 35 lakh Lakpati Didis across 7,000 blocks nationwide under the National Rural Livelihood
Mission.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 63 GVK Study Circle

• Each selected woman receives four laptops via Common Service Centres (CSCs), with furniture loan
support through SERP.

in government programs, along with digital training, permissions, and incentives.
• Panchayat-level retail stores under “Didi Ka Dukan” receive bank loans ranging from ₹50,000 to ₹2 lakh
for inventory purchases.
• In Tadikonda Mandal, 15 beneficiaries across 15 panchayats have been selected; 6 CSCs are operational,
and 50 retail shops have been established with 50 beneficiaries.

AI4 AP Police – AI Integration in Policing
• Andhra Pradesh Police piloted “AI4 AP Police,” an AI-based system in Chittoor, Guntur, and Annamayya
districts to expand technology in policing.
• DGP Harish Kumar Gupta launched it at Mangalagiri Police Headquarters, planning statewide expansion
within six months.
• Tools were developed from AI4 AP Police hackathon held in June 2025.
• Eight AI modules: complaint reception, digital evidence analysis, social media monitoring, petition
review, telecom/banking notices, investigation co-pilot for BNS/POCSO/IT law compliance, OCR for
paper records, and news tracking.
• Petition AI reduced petition review time from 30–45 minutes to 5–10 minutes.
• Digital Evidence AI automates notices and tracks actions.
• Cognitive Net AI analyzes call data and bank statements for investigators.
• Docs-to-Data converts paper records into digital knowledge base via AI OCR.
• Social Intelligence AI converts WhatsApp/social media content into actionable intelligence.
• News-360 AI monitors, translates, and summarizes state and district news.
• AI Foundation ensures data security and output stability; expected to improve investigation speed,
evidence analysis, and public policing services.
• Andhra Pradesh Police — DGP: Harish Kumar Gupta; Headquarters: Mangalagiri.
• Andhra Pradesh Capital: Amaravati; Chief Minister: N. Chandrababu Naidu; Governor: S. Abdul Nazeer.

Andhra Yatri – Government Urban Transport App (Andhra Pradesh)
• The Andhra Pradesh Transport Department plans to launch the “Andhra Yatri” app for safe, affordable
urban transport using a PPP model.
• Designed as an alternative to private auto, taxi, and bike taxi apps; operations managed via tendered private
operator.
• Drivers pay nominal subscription per ride Bike ₹3, Auto ₹5, Car ₹11; unlimited rides if daily ₹30–₹50 is
paid.
• No commissions like private apps; 10% of collected fees remitted to government for branding/promotional
use.
• Coordination with Police, APSRTC, Tourism, Labour Department, RTGS systems; real-time tracking
ensures safety and low fares.
• Initially launched in 13 district headquarters, including Vijayawada and Tirupati, with gradual expansion;
potential future integration with central “Bharat Yatri” app.

‘Cyber Sarathi’ AI Voice Agent for Cybercrime Complaints – Telangana
• Telangana Cyber Security Bureau launched ‘Cyber Sarathi’, an AI voice agent for cybercrime victims;
first AI voice service in the country integrated with the cybercrime complaint system.
• Aims to reduce call waiting issues when calling the National Cyber Crime Helpline 1930; launched in
February 2026 and will be fully operational soon.
• Calls from Telangana to 1930 are routed to the Telangana Cyber Security Bureau call center; 15 staff
operate in three shifts, handling around 1,400 calls daily; each complaint takes approximately 20 minutes
to register.
• When call agents are busy, calls are automatically routed to ‘Cyber Sarathi’, which listens, asks questions,
collects details, and logs complaints.
• Subsequently, digital evidence like bank transaction screenshots and messages are submitted via
WhatsApp; AI completes the complaint registration process.
• Telangana Cyber Security Bureau State-level cybercrime control agency; conducts prevention,
investigation, and awareness programs.
• National Cyber Crime Helpline Number: 1930; purpose: immediate assistance to cybercrime victims;
nationwide coverage.
• National Cyber Crime Reporting Portal online platform for registering cybercrime complaints; managed
by Government of India.

strengthens cyber-financial security.

Telangana Leads in Farmer Registration Process

installments.

Telangana Food Security Expansion (2025–2026)
• By 23 January 2026, Telangana’s ration cards reached 1,04,35,848, covering 3,35,96,875 beneficiaries.
• In the past year, 14,40,566 new cards were issued, and 46,153 cards were approved in January 2026 alone.
• As of 1 January 2025, there were 89,95,282 cards: 54,66,844 central food security cards and 35,28,438
state cards.
• By 23 January 2026, central cards increased to 56,57,001 and state cards to 47,78,848, reducing the gap
between the two categories.
• 54,49,310 beneficiaries were added with new cards; central cards serve 1,91,69,600 people, state cards
serve 1,44,27,275 people.
• Each of the 3.35 crore beneficiaries receives 6 kg of rice per month for free.
• National Food Security Act Implemented: 2013; Objective: Legal entitlement to subsidized grains;
enforced via Public Distribution System (PDS).
• Public Distribution System Government mechanism to supply essential commodities at subsidized rates
to eligible households.
• Telangana Capital: Hyderabad; Chief Minister: E. Revanth Reddy; Governor: J. Dev Varma.
Death of Telangana’s First Female Oggu Kathakaarini Mallari
• Telangana’s first female Oggu Kathakaarini, Mallari (80), passed away due to illness in Medipalli village,
Yacharam Mandal, Rangareddy district.
• Daughter of the traditional Oggu storyteller Gundala, she started assisting her father at age 12.
• From age 16, she performed independently, donning the traditional male attire of panche and chokka,
presenting Oggu folk narratives.
• She held hundreds of performances across the state, promoting traditional folk art to the public.
• Over seven decades, she dedicated her life to art and received the Telangana Government’s Distinguished
Women Award in 2020.
• Oggu Kathalu Traditional Telangana folk narrative art; stories often feature Shaiva deities like Mallanna
and Beerappa; traditionally performed by men.
• Telangana Capital: Hyderabad; Chief Minister: E. Revanth Reddy; Governor: J. Dev Varma.

Alwar District Becomes India’s First District with 1 00% Insurance Coverage
• On 3 February 2026, Alwar district in Rajasthan became the first district in India to achieve 100%
insurance coverage, aligning with the national goal “Insurance for All by 2047.”
• The initiative was implemented in coordination with the Government of India and the Insurance
Regulatory and Development Authority of India (IRDAI).
• Initially implemented in nine villages, every eligible adult was brought under insurance coverage.
• Household surveys enrolled 1,367 new individuals above 10 years of age, achieving complete coverage in
the district.
• Villages fully covered include Bhulla Ka Bass, Bichpuri, Palankheda, Antpada, Hanumant, Kalyanpur,
Morodkhurd, Kodalka, and Lalpura.
• Premiums for all eligible beneficiaries were paid by a voluntary organization, providing financial
protection without any burden on them.
• The insurance policies were issued by United India Insurance Company Limited.

NHAI Launches India’s First ‘Bee Corridors’ Program
• The National Highways Authority of India (NHAI) launched the country’s first “Bee Corridors” program.
• The program aims to replace decorative plants along highways with environmentally friendly green
corridors to protect bees and other pollinators.
• Focuses on supporting pollination services, agricultural productivity, and reducing pressure on
biodiversity.
• Flowering plants are planted sequentially throughout the year to maintain continuous pollination
environments.
• Approximately one-third of the world’s food crops depend on pollination.
• Long connected habitats are established along highways, planting flowering clusters 500 meters to 1
kilometer apart.
• NHAI Established: 1988; Headquarters: New Delhi; Administrative Ministry: Ministry of Road Transport
and Highways.
• Pollinators Bees, butterflies, birds, and bats that are crucial for crop pollination.
• Sustainable infrastructure — Development integrated with environmental conservation.
India’s First “Musical Road” in Mumbai (500 meters ‘Jai Ho’ Tune)
• A 500-meter-long musical road was created on the northbound lane of Mumbai Coastal Road from
Nariman Point to Worli, where vehicles moving at 70–80 km/h play the ‘Jai Ho’ tune through road
grooves/rumble strips.
• The sound is produced by the friction and vibrations of tires over grooves; the design ensures the tune is
audible inside vehicles.
• Maharashtra Chief Minister Devendra Fadnavis inaugurated the musical road, located between
Priyadarshini Park and Amarsions Garden after the tunnel exit.
• Advance signboards were installed for prior information.
• BMC Implemented the project at an estimated cost of ₹6.21 crore, with technical support from Hungarian
experts.
• Promoting consistent driving speed enhances road safety; there are plans to implement musical roads at
more locations in the future.
• Brihanmumbai Municipal Corporation (BMC) Headquarters: Mumbai; Nature: Municipal Corporation;
Current Municipal Commissioner: Bhushan Gagrani.
• Global examples Melody Roads in Japan, Singing Road in South Korea, Musical Road in UAE.
• Japan Capital: Tokyo; Currency: Yen; Prime Minister: Sanae Takaichi.
• South Korea Capital: Seoul; Currency: Won; President: Lee Jae-myung.

Minister: Mohammed bin Rashid Al Maktoum.

Implementation of “World’s Largest Grain Storage Plan” in Balaghat
• Balaghat district in Madhya Pradesh was selected as a pilot district under the cooperative sector’s World’s
Largest Grain Storage Plan (WLGS).
• A 500-metric ton warehouse was constructed at the Bahudeshiya Primary Effort Sakh Cooperative Society
in Parswada and inaugurated by the Prime Minister on 24 February 2024.
• The warehouse was later leased to the Madhya Pradesh Warehousing and Logistics Corporation.
• The plan aims to decentralize grain storage infrastructure at the Primary Agricultural Credit Society
(PACS) level.
• It is implemented in coordination with schemes such as the Agriculture Infrastructure Fund (AIF),
Agricultural Marketing Infrastructure (AMI), Sub-Mission on Agricultural Mechanization (SMAM), and
PM Formalisation of Micro Food Processing Enterprises (PMFME).
• The WLGS Plan seeks to establish warehouses at the PACS level, increase storage capacity, reduce grain
loss, and ensure better prices for farmers.
• PACS Cooperative credit institutions operating at the village level; the plan aims to develop multi-service
centers.
• Agriculture Infrastructure Fund Provides financial support for agricultural infrastructure like warehouses,
processing, marketing, and mechanization.
• Madhya Pradesh Warehousing and Logistics Corporation State-level organization managing storage,
warehouses, and logistics.
• Madhya Pradesh Capital: Bhopal; Currency: Indian Rupee; Chief Minister: Mohan Yadav; Governor:
Mangubhai C. Patel.
Implementation of Anti-Drone System at V.O. Chidambaranar Port — India’s First
• V.O. Chidambaranar Port Authority became the first in India to deploy an advanced anti-drone system to
strengthen coastal and naval security.
• The system integrates radio-frequency and radar-based technologies, designed to operate effectively in
complex port environments.
• It provides 360-degree coverage with an operational range of up to 5 kilometers.
• Components include a drone detector, drone identification radar, and man-pack jammers, enabling real-
time detection, tracking, classification, and neutralization of unauthorized drones.
• The system enhances the protection of port assets, personnel, and operations, serving as a domestic
electronic countermeasure solution for coastal security.
• V.O. Chidambaranar Port Authority Location: Tuticorin, Tamil Nadu; Nature: Central Government Major
Port; Key role in South India maritime trade and security.
• Tamil Nadu Capital: Chennai; Chief Minister: M.K. Stalin; Governor: R.N. Ravi.

Kerala Comprehensive Urban Policy – 2026
• On 13 February 2026, Kerala became the first state in India to formulate and approve a Comprehensive
Urban Policy, designed as a long-term strategic plan to address rapidly growing urbanization,
infrastructure, sustainability, and urban governance.
• The policy, prepared by the Local Self Government Department (LSGD), was approved by the State
Cabinet chaired by Chief Minister Pinarayi Vijayan.
• The Kerala Urban Policy Commission estimated that by 2050, the state’s urban population would reach
around 80%.
• Key objectives include developing climate-smart cities, creating a continuous network of urban areas,
scientific planning, good governance, and enhancing climate resilience.
• Planned urbanization in environmentally sensitive areas aims to promote economic growth, integrated
development, reduce social and regional inequalities, and improve urban infrastructure.
• The policy also focuses on strengthening institutional frameworks, local self-governance, citizen-centric
services, and strategic planning implementation.
• Kerala — Capital: Thiruvananthapuram; Chief Minister: Pinarayi Vijayan; Governor: Rajendra Arlekar.
• Kerala Urban Policy Commission — Established to plan state-level urban development strategies.
Kerala’s First Comprehensive Graphene Policy
• In February 2026, Kerala became the first state in India to approve a comprehensive Graphene Policy
under the chairmanship of Chief Minister Pinarayi Vijayan, aiming to position the state as a global hub for
graphene research, development, and production.
• Incentives include a 50% reduction in lease charges for graphene units in industrial parks, payment in five
installments, up to 45% capital expenditure support, exemptions on stamp duty, registration fees, and
electricity tariffs.
• A Graphene Innovation Center will be set up near Kalamassery, Kochi, with an investment of ₹87 crore.
• The ‘Graphene Aurora’ project by Digital University Kerala in Palakkad is supported jointly by the
Ministry of Electronics & IT, the state government, and industry partners.
• Collaboration with foreign universities such as Oxford and Manchester is planned to commercialize
research outcomes.
• Graphene a two-dimensional carbon layer one atom thick, known for high strength, electrical and thermal
conductivity, surface area, and application in electronics, sensors, energy storage, composites, coatings,
and water–air filtration.

Kashiwazaki–Kariwa Nuclear Power Plant Recommissioned in Japan
• Japan recommissioned the Kashiwazaki–Kariwa Nuclear Power Plant, recognized as the world’s largest
nuclear power facility, operated by Tokyo Electric Power Company (TEPCO).
• Operations had been halted previously due to technical and safety reasons.
• After the 2011 earthquake and tsunami caused the Fukushima nuclear disaster, Japan shut down several
nuclear power plants nationwide.
• To reduce dependence on fossil fuels and achieve carbon neutrality by 2050, Japan is increasing nuclear
power usage again.
• Considering rising electricity demand driven by AI and digital sectors, the plant has been restarted.
• The plant has 7 reactors; one reactor resumed operations at 2 PM; after full safety checks, the entire facility
will become fully operational.
• Tokyo Electric Power Company (TEPCO) Established: 1951; Headquarters: Tokyo; Nature: Electricity
generation and supply; Operator of Fukushima Daiichi Nuclear Plant.
• Kashiwazaki–Kariwa Nuclear Power Plant Location: Niigata Prefecture, Japan; Distinction: World’s
largest nuclear power plant; Reactors: 7.
• Japan Capital: Tokyo; Currency: Japanese Yen; Prime Minister: Sanae Takaichi; Goal: Net-zero carbon
emissions by 2050.
Denmark Eliminates Mother-to-Child HIV and Syphilis Transmission
• On 27 February 2026, the World Health Organization (WHO) certified Denmark as the first country in the
European Union to eliminate mother-to-child transmission of HIV and syphilis.
• This achievement was based on targets set between 2021–2024, ensuring at least 95% of pregnant women
received testing and treatment, resulting in fewer than 50 infant transmissions per 100,000 live births.
• Regular testing and timely medical interventions have brought transmission rates close to zero, with a
robust primary healthcare system and universal health coverage cited as key factors.
• Denmark is now moving forward with a triple elimination goal, including hepatitis B alongside HIV and
syphilis.
• World Health Organization Headquarters: Geneva, Switzerland; Founded: 7 April 1948; Director-General:
Dr. Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus; UN specialized health agency.
• Denmark Capital: Copenhagen; Currency: Danish Krone; Monarch: King Frederik X; Prime Minister:
Mette Frederiksen.
• HIV — Human Immunodeficiency Virus; Syphilis — Bacterial infection.

developed by U.S.-based Vast Space.
• It marks a key transition from government-managed space infrastructure to private operation, with
commercial space stations expected to play a major role after the International Space Station is deorbited
in 2030.

India’s First Hydrogen-Powered Agricultural Drone
• Haryana-based startup Ankur developed India’s first hydrogen-powered agricultural drone, showcased at
the Krishi Darshan Expo in Hisar.
• Using 3 kg of hydrogen fuel, the drone can stay airborne continuously for ~3 hours, spray pesticides, and
carry loads up to 50 kg.
• Unlike battery-powered drones, it avoids frequent charging, reduces farmers’ exposure to chemicals, and
emits no pollutants.
• While battery drones require 200–250 liters of water per acre, this hydrogen drone needs only 10 liters.
• Equipped with multispectral sensors, it can detect pests, diseases, crop health, and nutrient deficiencies.
• Hydrogen fuel Clean energy source producing only water vapor as a byproduct.
• Hydrogen fuel cell Electrochemical device that converts chemical energy directly into electricity.
• Precision agriculture Technology-driven farming using sensors, GPS, AI, and drones.
• Haryana Capital: Chandigarh; Chief Minister: Manohar Lal Khattar; Governor: Bandaru Dattatreya.
AIIMS New Delhi Launches India’s First Face Transplant Program
• AIIMS New Delhi has become the first hospital in India to announce a face transplant program.
• The Plastic, Reconstructive, and Burns Surgery Department has initiated a special registry for patients
with severe facial deformities and loss of critical functions such as breathing or eyelid movement.
• Face transplantation involves replacing facial tissue from a deceased donor to restore facial structure and
function through complex surgery.
• Globally, around 80 face transplants have been performed; the first-ever face transplant was conducted in
France in 2005, with recent procedures in Spain.
• The program emphasizes ethical review, psychological counseling, and multidisciplinary coordination.
• AIIMS New Delhi Established: 1956; Headquarters: New Delhi; Nature: Nationally important medical
research institution.

transplantation.

India’s First National Biobank for Lysosomal Storage Disorders (LSDs)
• In February 2026, the Government of India inaugurated the country’s first government-supported national
biobank for Lysosomal Storage Disorders (LSDs) in Ahmedabad, Gujarat.
• The biobank is established at the Institute of Human Genetics within the Foundation for Research in
Genetics and Endocrinology (FRIGE).
• Its primary objective is to create a centralized national repository for research on rare genetic disorders.
• The biobank collects and stores biological samples such as DNA, blood, plasma, urine, and clinical data.
It houses samples and clinical-genomic data from 530 patients collected over 17 years (2008–2025).
• LSDs are rare inherited metabolic disorders where enzyme deficiencies cause harmful substances to
accumulate in cells, affecting organs such as the brain, liver, spleen, and bones.
• The biobank facilitates advanced research, development of new diagnostic methods, drug targets, and
treatment strategies.
• Collaborative programs with Tata Institute for Genetics and Society (TIGS) in Bengaluru aim to develop
human stem cell-based disease models.
• FRIGE Location: Ahmedabad, Gujarat; Nature: Genetics and endocrine disease research institute.
• TIGS Established: 2020; Headquarters: Bengaluru; Nature: Genetics and biotechnology research institute.
• Gujarat Capital: Gandhinagar; Chief Minister: Bhupendrabhai Patel; Governor: Acharya Devvrat.
• India Capital: New Delhi; Currency: Indian Rupee; Prime Minister: Narendra Modi; Union Health
Minister: Jagat Prakash Nadda.

World’s First Doctor-Led AI Home Healthcare System ‘iLive Connect’
• In February 2026, India launched ‘iLive Connect,’ the world’s first doctor-led AI home healthcare system,
providing 24×7 ICU-level monitoring from home.
• Developed by Dr. Rahul Chandroda and Dr. Vivek Kumar, the system offers personalized continuous
medical care.
• It uses FDA- and CE-certified wearable devices (chest biosensor patches, wristbands) to continuously
track critical parameters including two-lead ECG, heart response, respiration rate, oxygen levels, body
temperature, blood pressure trends, physical activity, and heart rate variability.
• Collected data is securely sent to the cloud and monitored 24/7 by experts at a central medical command
center.

World’s Longest Snake
• A massive female reticulated python discovered in the forests of Sulawesi, Indonesia, has been certified
by Guinness World Records as the longest snake in the world.
• Scientific name: Malayopython reticulatus; length: 7.22 meters (23 feet 8 inches); nickname: “Ibu Baron
(Baroness).”
• Its length is roughly equal to the width of a FIFA-standard goalpost.
• While fully relaxed, its length could potentially be about 10% longer, but measurements were taken with
animal welfare in mind.
• The python was identified in December 2025 in Maros Regency, Sulawesi Island.

130-member Congress.
• General elections in Peru are scheduled for April 12; frequent presidential changes have occurred due to
impeachment and corruption allegations.
• Peru Capital: Lima; Currency: Peruvian Sol; President: José María Balkazar.
• Peru Congress Unicameral Legislature; Members: 130.
• Peru Location: Western South America; Neighboring countries: Ecuador, Colombia, Brazil, Bolivia,

from June 21, 1948, to January 26, 1950.

system in India.
• This initiative is part of efforts to remove colonial remnants and honor India’s cultural heritage.

• 17-year-old Aditya Pandya completed a simulated lunar habitat mission under moon-like conditions and

• The mission was conducted from February 1 to 8, 2026, in Dholavira, Kutch district, Gujarat.
• The program was organized by AAKA Space Studio, a company focused on space research and simulation
activities.
• The mission was designed to study the challenges of isolation and task management faced in real space
missions.
• Dholavira UNESCO World Heritage Site since July 27, 2021; an ancient Harappan civilization site.

isolation studies; Concordia Station (Antarctica) – used for space-analog research under extreme cold and
isolation conditions.

Satendra Singh Lohia Swims Cook Strait
• 38-year-old Indian para-swimmer Satendra Singh Lohia successfully swam New Zealand’s Cook Strait in
9 hours and 22 minutes.
• Cook Strait is considered one of the most challenging open-water sea routes in the world due to
unpredictable weather, strong currents, and extremely cold water.
• He covered a distance of approximately 23.6 kilometers.
• Satendra Singh Lohia is recognized as the first Asian para-swimmer to successfully cross Cook Strait.
• Since 1962, over 100 swimmers have crossed Cook Strait, but the extreme conditions limit the number of
successful attempts.
• In 2024, the Government of India awarded Satendra Singh Lohia the Padma Shri.
• Lohia has participated 11 times in national Paralympic swimming competitions, winning 4 gold and 23
medals; in 3 international Paralympic swimming events, he earned 1 gold, 2 silver, and 1 bronze medal.
• Cook Strait lies between the North and South Islands of New Zealand, connecting the Tasman Sea and the
South Pacific Ocean.
• This swim was part of the “Oceans Seven” challenge in international marathon swimming.
• New Zealand Capital: Wellington; Currency: New Zealand Dollar; Prime Minister: Christopher Luxon.
• Madhya Pradesh Capital: Bhopal; Chief Minister: Mohan Yadav; Governor: Mangubhai C. Patel.
• Padma Shri Instituted: 1954; India’s fourth-highest civilian award.

Internet Pioneer David J. Farber Passes Away
• David J. Farber passed away at the age of 91 in Tokyo due to heart failure.
• He was regarded as the "grandfather of the internet."
• Beginning his career at Bell Laboratories in the 1950s, he conducted pioneering research on integrating
computing systems with telecommunications.
• Under Farber’s guidance, foundational principles of modern networking were established, mentoring
pioneers who built the global internet architecture.
• Since 2018, he had been teaching at Keio University.
• Bell Laboratories Established: 1925; Headquarters: New Jersey, USA; Nature: Telecommunications
research institution.
• Keio University Established: 1858; Headquarters: Tokyo, Japan; Nature: Private research university.
• ARPANET Launched in 1969; World Wide Web invented by Tim Berners-Lee in 1989.

• Japan Capital: Tokyo; Currency: Yen; Prime Minister: Sanae Takaichi.

• Celebrated Bengali writer Mani Shankar Mukherjee, popularly known as Shankar, passed away at the age
of 92 in Kolkata.
• He was a recipient of the Sahitya Akademi Award and a literary figure whose work influenced modern
Bengali literature for over seven decades.
• Shankar made his literary debut in 1955 with the work Kata Ajanare and gained acclaim for novels like

based on his writings.

claiming the championship for the sixth time.
• The final was held in Harare, Zimbabwe, where India defeated England by a margin of 100 runs, setting
the highest winning margin in an Under-19 final.
• Batting first, India scored 411 runs for the loss of 9 wickets in 50 overs.
• Fourteen-year-old Vaibhav Suryavanshi starred with 175 runs off 80 balls, while captain Ayush Mhatre
contributed a crucial 53 runs.

IITS
IIT Kanpur 3D Data-Driven Solar Activity Model
• IIT Kanpur researchers developed a 3D data-driven model to predict solar activity, a world-first effort.
• The model supports space weather monitoring and early warning systems.
• Solar storms are controlled by the strength and weakness of the solar interior dynamo process.
• The solar activity cycle typically lasts 11 years, including Solar Maximum and Minimum phases.
• Solar storms can release high-energy charged particles and magnetic fields, potentially disrupting satellite
operations, communication, and navigation on Earth.
• IIT Kanpur Established: 1959; Headquarters: Kanpur, Uttar Pradesh.
IIT Kanpur’s ‘Saathi’ Platform Gains International Recognition
• IIT Kanpur developed Saathi (Self-Assessment, Test and Help for Entrance Exams), an AI-based
educational platform, officially supported by India’s Ministry of Education.
• Highlighted at India AI Impact Summit 2026 in the casebook “Real World Impact of AI in Education.”
• Provides personalized guidance to students in rural and underprivileged areas, with AI-driven study
planning and assessment.
• IIT Kanpur Established: 1959; Director: Prof. Manindra Agrawal.
• Ministry of Education Cabinet Minister: Dharmendra Pradhan.
AI Technology for River Flow Forecasting – IIT Delhi
• IIT Delhi researchers integrated AI with traditional hydrological models to accurately forecast flows for
208 rivers and tributaries in India.
• Benefits include improved water release scheduling, flood management, and reservoir operations.
• Model trained with data from 220 river gauge stations over the past 20 years.
• AI system overcomes uncertainties due to local data gaps in conventional models.
• IIT Delhi – Established: 1961; Location: New Delhi.
IIT Ropar – Defence Technology M.Tech Program with Indian Army
• IIT Ropar and the Indian Army launched an M.Tech program in Defence Technology for officers of the
Armed Corps Center & School.
• Designed to enhance officers’ technical and operational expertise; first-of-its-kind IIT program partnered

• Integrates academic research, applied technology, and military experience to strengthen national defence
capabilities.
• Supports Atmanirbhar Bharat (Self-Reliant India) goals.

for green hydrogen production.
• Research duration: ~18 months, focusing on cost-effective water electrolysis catalysts.
• The electrode uses cheap and abundant elements: Molybdenum, Vanadium, Sulfur, Carbon.

HISTORY-HERITAGE-CULTURE
Mandatory “Vande Mataram” in Official Programs and Schools
• On 6 February 2026, the Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA) issued guidelines mandating that the song
“Vande Mataram” with all six stanzas be performed during official government functions and in
educational institutions.
• The complete rendition should last 3 minutes and 10 seconds.
• Individuals present must stand attentively while singing or playing the song.
• It is not mandatory to stand during its play in cinema halls.
• At the national level, it must be performed during flag parades, civil awards ceremonies, and events
attended by the President.
• Before and after broadcasts of the President’s speeches on All India Radio and Doordarshan, “Vande
Mataram” must be played.
• At the state level, it must be performed when Governors or Lieutenant Governors arrive at or leave official
events.
• Schools are instructed to include “Vande Mataram” in morning assemblies.
• When both “Vande Mataram” and the National Anthem are played at a single program, “Vande Mataram”
should be performed first.
• This decision commemorates 150 years since the first publication of “Vande Mataram” on 7 November
1875.
100th Anniversary Celebrations of Ol Chiki Script
• On 16 February 2026, the Ministry of Culture organized the centenary celebrations of the Ol Chiki script
in New Delhi.
• The government issued a commemorative coin and a postage stamp for the occasion.
• Ol Chiki was developed in 1925 by Pandit Raghunath Murmu for the Santali language.
• Santali was included in the Eighth Schedule of the Indian Constitution in 2003.
• The script has 30 characters representing Santali phonetics.
• Santali A Munda language of the Austro-Asiatic family, primarily spoken in Jharkhand, Odisha, West
Bengal, and Assam.
• Eighth Schedule of the Constitution Initially included 14 languages, now 22.
• Pandit Raghunath Murmu Cultural reformer and playwright, revered as “Guru Gomke” in the Santali
community.
• Ministry of Culture Headquarters: New Delhi; Current Minister: Gajendra Singh Shekhawat.

Exhibition of Buddha’s Devnimori Relics in Sri Lanka
• India began exhibiting the sacred Buddha Devnimori relics in Sri Lanka from 4 February, as part of cultural
and spiritual diplomacy.
• The relics were discovered at Devnimori archaeological site in Aravalli district, Gujarat.
• Devnimori was first studied by archaeologist Prof. S. N. Chowdhury in 1957.
• Excavations revealed evidence of Buddhism’s spread in western India.
• A 24-foot-high green schist relic casket with Brahmi inscription “Dashabala Sharira Nilaya” (meaning
“Abode of Buddha’s Body Relics”) was found in the Devnimori stupa.
• These are among the oldest authenticated Buddhist relics discovered in India.
• The exhibition strengthens India–Sri Lanka spiritual and cultural ties.
• Archaeological Survey of India Established: 1861; Headquarters: New Delhi; Director General: Yadubeer
Singh Rawat.
Konark Sun Temple Sanctum Reopens After 122 Years
• The 13th-century Konark Sun Temple in Odisha will reopen its sanctum after 122 years.
• In 1903–04, the British filled the sanctum with thousands of tons of sand to prevent the collapse of a 15-
foot-high wall.
• Jagamohan Hall remained closed for over a century.
• The Archaeological Survey of India, in collaboration with IIT Madras experts, began the sand removal
process.
• Removing all sand will take approximately three months, followed by structural and stability assessment.
• Decisions on reconstruction and devotee access will follow the inspection.
• Konark Sun Temple Constructed around 1250 CE by King Narasimhadeva I; recognized as a UNESCO
World Heritage Site in 1984.
• Odisha Capital: Bhubaneswar; Chief Minister: Mohan Charan Majhi.
• Archaeological Survey of India — Established: 1861; Director General: Yadubeer Singh Rawat.
• IIT Madras Established: 1959; Location: Chennai.
Chennakeshava Temple (Belur) Inspires Seva Teerth Design
• The Prime Minister noted that the new office complex “Seva Teerth” was constructed following principles
of Indian Vastu Shastra, drawing inspiration from the 12th-century Chennakeshava Temple in Belur.
• The Chennakeshava Temple, also called Keshava Temple or Vijayanarayana Temple, is a 12th-century
Hindu temple dedicated to Lord Vishnu (Chennakeshava) located in Belur, Hassan district, Karnataka.
• Built by Hoysala King Vishnuvardhana in 1117 CE along the Yagachi River, the temple’s construction is
linked to his military victories around 1116 CE, reflecting the notion of “victory” in historical accounts.
• The temple complex expanded over three generations, with construction spanning approximately 103
years, showcasing intricate Hoysala architectural style, detailed carvings, and terracotta sculpture work.
• In 2023, the Chennakeshava Temple was included in UNESCO’s World Heritage listing under the
“Hoysala Sacred Ensembles.”
• Other temples in the UNESCO “Hoysala Sacred Ensembles” include Hoysaleswara Temple (Halebidu)
and Keshava Temple (Somanathapura).
• Location: Belur, Hassan district, Karnataka; River: Yagachi River.
• Hoysala Empire period and temple expansion: 12th century.

Medieval Temple Remains in Jhunjhunu, Rajasthan
• Excavations near Tyonda village, Jhunjhunu district, Rajasthan, conducted by the Archaeological Survey
of India (ASI) uncovered medieval temple remains in February 2026.
• Sculptural fragments of Goddess Lakshmi and Lord Ganesha were unearthed.
• Preliminary dating suggests these remains belong to the 11th–12th centuries CE.
• Based on architectural style and sculptural features, the temple is believed to date back to the Rajput period
or even earlier.
• This discovery aids the study of medieval North-West Indian history and temple architecture.
• Each artifact is being documented, photographed, categorized, and preserved according to scientific
methods.
• After detailed analysis and precise dating, the region’s historical significance will be better understood.
• ASI Established: 1861; Headquarters: New Delhi; Director General: Yadubeer Singh Rawat.
Indus Valley Civilization May Be ~8,000 Years Old
• Recent radiocarbon dating at Bhirrana (Northern India) suggests the Indus Valley Civilization (IVC) may
be older than previously estimated.
• The results indicate organized settlement/residential activity may have begun around ~8,000 years ago.
• The oldest sample at Bhirrana yielded an average radiocarbon age of 8.35 ± 0.14 ka BP, suggesting a range
of ~8597–8171 years BP.
• Linking this early settlement phase to Indus cultural evolution implies that urban civilization emerged
gradually from agrarian communities rather than instantaneously.
• Typically, the mature/nuclear urban phase of the IVC is placed between 2600–1900 BCE, but broader
discussions exist regarding its cultural span.
• If early habitation dates are accepted, the timeline would predate the start of Egypt’s dynastic period
(~3100 BCE).
• Evidence from major sites like Harappa and Mohenjo-Daro — including grid-pattern streets, water
facilities, and sealed drainage systems — indicates advanced urban planning and sanitation.
• Standardized weights, seals, and trade indicators reflect complex administrative and commercial
organization.
• Due to its extensive reach, the Indus Civilization is considered one of the earliest and most widespread
ancient civilizations.
• Bhirrana Location: Fatehabad district, Haryana; connected to seasonal Ghaggar river channels with
multiple cultural layers.
• Haryana Significant Indus sites: Rakhigarhi (Hisar district), Bhirrana, Banawali, Farmana.
• “BP” in radiocarbon dating denotes “Before Present,” with “Present” standardized to 1950 CE.

Tamil-Brahmi Inscriptions Discovered in Egypt
• Researchers reported nearly 30 Indian inscriptions at the Valley of the Kings in Egypt, primarily in Tamil-
Brahmi (Tamili), Prakrit, and Sanskrit scripts, dating from 1st–3rd century CE.
• These inscriptions are located in six tombs in the Theban Necropolis near the Nile, suggesting Indian
visitors and merchants traveled beyond Egyptian coastal areas into the interior.
• The Tamil name Cikai Korran appears eight times across five tombs, indicating repeated visits or the
marking of identity by a single Tamil individual.
• Other Tamil names like Kopān, Cātan, and Kiran were also identified, hinting at Sangam-era Tamil
cultural connections.
• Similar Tamil inscriptions were previously found at the Berenike port on the Red Sea, highlighting strong
Indo-Roman maritime trade links.
• Some reports suggest tomb construction dates around 1600 BCE, but the Indian inscriptions have been
dated to 1st–3rd century CE, implying they were added later by travelers.
• Egypt Capital: Cairo; Currency: Egyptian Pound (EGP); President: Abdel Fattah el-Sisi.
• Valley of the Kings — Theban Necropolis near Luxor, Nile region; prominent ancient Egyptian royal
burial site.
900-Year-Old Chola-Era Shiva Temple Unearthed Beneath Mango Tree Roots
• A nearly 900-year-old Shiva temple from the Chola period (1070–1122 CE, reign of Kulottunga Chola I)
was recently discovered in Tamil Nadu.
• The temple was buried under the roots of a 200-year-old mango tree in Begili village near Hosur,
Krishnagiri district.
• Locals previously referred to this site as the “Pandava temple,” unaware of its historical significance.
• Chola dynasty temples are known for granite construction, symmetrical design, and intricate sculptures.
• Chola rulers predominantly patronized Shaivism.
• Tamil Nadu Capital: Chennai; Chief Minister: M. K. Stalin; Governor: R. N. Ravi.
Bhairava Sculpture Identified at Raichakonda Fort, Telangana
• Telangana’s new history team identified a 9-foot-high Bhairava sculpture at Raichakonda Fort, carved
among Ganapati sculptures in Bhogandani Mandapa.
• The sculpture depicts Bhairava in a dancing posture, holding a drum and trident in upper hands, a
sacrificial sword and blood vessel in lower hands, a severed head, with blood dripping, and a devotee
worshipping nearby.
• Researchers believe the sculpture reflects the Raichakonda Velama military traditions.
• Raichakonda Fort Yadadri Bhuvanagiri district; built by Recher rulers in the 14th century.
• Bhairava Fierce form of Lord Shiva, associated with Kalabhairava.
• Telangana Capital: Hyderabad; Chief Minister: A. Revanth Reddy; Governor: Jishnu Dev Varma.

Serengsia Battle (1837) — Tribal Rebellion Against British Rule
• The 1837 Serengsia battle was a key tribal resistance led by the Ho community against British expansion,
recognized by the Jharkhand government.
• Location: Serengsia valley, West Singhbhum district, Jharkhand.
• It was one of the first organized armed rebellions by tribal groups against British authority in eastern India.
• Context: British annexation of Kolhan in 1820–21 and the 1831 Kol revolt escalated, leading to the
Serengsia battle in 1837.
• The rebellion intensified due to British occupation of Ho villages and oppression by zamindars and
officials.
• Jharkhand Capital: Ranchi; Chief Minister: Hemant Soren; Governor: Santosh Kumar Gangwar.

India Assumes Chairmanship of the Indian Ocean Naval Symposium
• The Indian Ocean Naval Symposium (IONS) was established in 2008 as a voluntary, inclusive, and
multilateral maritime platform to promote cooperation among navies of the Indian Ocean Region.
• India served as the first chair from 2008 to 2010, followed by the United Arab Emirates, South Africa,
Australia, Bangladesh, Iran, France, and Thailand.
• In 2026, India assumed the chairmanship again, renewing focus on regional maritime security.
• The forum currently includes 25 member countries from the Indian Ocean Region.
• It functions through discussions at the level of naval chiefs and specialized working groups.
• The objective of the platform is to ensure peace and stability, secure sea lanes, coordinate humanitarian
assistance and disaster relief operations, address maritime security challenges, and promote professional
exchanges and joint exercises.

Prahaar – India’s First Comprehensive National Counter-Terrorism Policy
• India announced its first comprehensive counter-terrorism policy named “Prahaar”, which formalizes a
proactive and intelligence-driven counter-terror doctrine.
• It serves as India’s first integrated national counter-terrorism strategy designed to prevent, respond to, and
recover from terrorist threats.
• The policy coordinates intelligence agencies, law enforcement bodies, technological platforms, and
international cooperation mechanisms.
• It focuses on disrupting terrorist financing, recruitment networks, radicalization processes, logistics
systems, and cyber networks.
• The initiative aims to strengthen India’s internal security architecture.

Successful Test of Solid Fuel Ducted Ramjet (SFDR) Technology
• India successfully tested a missile propulsion system powered by Solid Fuel Ducted Ramjet (SFDR)
technology from the Integrated Test Range off the Odisha coast.
• The test validated key technology required for developing next-generation long-range air-to-air missiles
capable of intercepting aerial targets.
• With this capability, India joins a select group of countries possessing advanced ramjet propulsion
technology.
• The missile generates continuous thrust by using atmospheric oxygen through its ramjet engine.
• This technology makes it extremely difficult for enemy fighter aircraft to evade interception.
• Defence Research and Development Organisation (DRDO) Established: 1958; Headquarters: New Delhi;
Supported by Research Centre Imarat and High Energy Materials Research Laboratory.

Operation Kiya – Joint Counter-Terror Operation in Jammu and Kashmir
• Operation Kiya is a joint counter-terrorism operation launched by Indian security forces to neutralize
terrorists hiding in forest and mountainous regions of Jammu and Kashmir.
• The operation began after intense encounters in the Basantgarh forest region of Udhampur district.
• Forces involved include the White Knight Corps, Counter-Insurgency Force, and Jammu and Kashmir
Police.
• The objective is to eliminate terrorists, block their movements, and ensure the safety of civilian areas.
• Through cordon-and-search operations, security forces have sealed escape routes in dense forest and
mountainous terrain.
• The operation reflects India’s intelligence-based and area-domination approach to counter-terrorism.
Successful Test of Very Short-Range Air Defence Missile System
• India successfully tested a Very Short-Range Air Defence (VSHORAD) missile system at Chandipur on
the Odisha coast.
• The missile successfully intercepted aerial targets at varying speeds, ranges, and altitudes.
• During the test, the missile successfully shot down airborne drones.
• The system is designed as a shoulder-fired weapon capable of countering low-altitude aerial threats.
• This capability enhances the combat readiness of the Indian armed forces against close-range air threats.
• Defence Research and Development Organisation (DRDO) — Established: 1958; Headquarters: New

ISRO Finalizes Landing Site for Chandrayaan-4 Mission
• The Indian Space Research Organisation (ISRO) has finalized the landing site for the Chandrayaan-4
mission.
• The mission is planned to land in the south polar region of the Moon.
• Chandrayaan-4 is designed as a sample-return mission aimed at collecting lunar samples and bringing
them back to Earth.
• The mission is targeted for launch by 2028.
• Four possible landing sites were examined in the Mons Mouton region of the Moon, and the MM-4 site
has been selected.
• The selection was based on images captured by the OHRC (Orbiter High Resolution Camera).
• ISRO Established: 1969; Headquarters: Bengaluru; Chairman: V. Narayanan.

communication and control to the payload.

China Develops High-Power Microwave Weapon
• A research team led by Wang Gang at the Northwest Institute of Nuclear Technology in China has
developed a high-power microwave weapon.
• The system is referred to as TPG-1000C.
• It can generate powerful microwave waves from the ground capable of disrupting electronic systems of
satellites in Low Earth Orbit (LEO).
• The device is about four metres long, weighs around five tonnes and can release about 20 gigawatts of
power for nearly one minute.
• The system may potentially be deployed from trucks, ships or space platforms.
• China has earlier expressed concerns that Starlink satellite networks could pose national security threats.
• Information about this weapon currently comes mainly from research reports.

ambitions.

platform.

programmes.

Wave-Propelled Vertical Profiler Developed by NIOT for Accurate Cyclone Prediction
• The National Institute of Ocean Technology (NIOT), Chennai, has developed a Wave-Propelled Vertical
Profiler (WVP) to improve the accuracy of cyclone prediction over oceans.
• The device uses ocean wave energy to move up and down in the water column while collecting data.
• The WVP performs the functions of about 12 sensors through a single integrated sensor system and is
attached to a wave rider buoy at a depth of about 55 metres.
• This technology will strengthen India’s coastal monitoring capabilities and early warning systems for
disasters.
• NIOT Established: 1993; Director: Dr. Balaji Ramakrishnan.
• Tamil Nadu Capital: Chennai; Chief Minister: M. K. Stalin.
Water from Air – Water Harvesting Technology Using Molecular Sponges
• Nobel laureate Omar Yaghi has developed an advanced device that can collect clean drinking water
directly from the air.
• The device uses molecular sponges to absorb moisture present in the atmosphere.
• It can collect at least 1,000 litres of purified water per day.
• The technology operates using solar energy and can function efficiently even in desert regions with
humidity levels below 20%.
• Since atmospheric moisture does not contain salts or minerals, the collected water is naturally pure.
• This technology could play a significant role in addressing global water scarcity in the future.
• Omar Yaghi is a prominent scientist in the fields of chemistry and materials science.
Photo-Rechargeable Supercapacitor Developed by DST Scientists
• Indian scientists have developed a solar-powered energy storage device called a photo-rechargeable
supercapacitor.
• Compared to conventional solar systems that rely on separate units for energy generation and storage, this
device reduces energy losses and improves efficiency.
www.youtube.com/@praveensir 112 GVK Study Circle
=== PDF PAGE 113 ===
• The system is portable and suitable for off-grid electricity applications.
• It uses binder-free nickel–cobalt oxide nanowires grown on nickel foam through an in-situ hydrothermal
process.
• This innovation could play a crucial role in future renewable energy storage technologies.
• Department of Science and Technology (DST) Established: 1971; Headquarters: New Delhi.
